## Sprint 2a — §16.2 verbatim

**Goal.** Insert sites write the new columns; coalescer and newer-inbound suppression are rekeyed with a dual-key transition; per-bot observability comes online; pre-S3 eval baseline captured. Mediator behavior unchanged. **No NOT NULL.**

Checklist file local to the sprint.

**Work items, ordered.**

1. Wire `routing.py` into `inbound.py:23-180`. Populate `TurnContext` with real scope fields (still mediator-only defaults).
2. Stamp insert sites:
   - `inbound.py:161` — `bot_id`, `topic_id`, `channel_id`.
   - `messaging.py:55, 84` — same on outbound.
   - `agentic.py:483` — `bot_id`, `topic_id`, `bot_spec_version` (hash of `BotSpec` repr), `hot_context_builder_version`, `tool_schema_version` (hash of `tool_schemas.py`).

Bursts in-flight at deploy flush under old key; new bursts use the composite key. Drop the legacy reader in S2b.

Dependencies: (1) gates (2–7); (8) runs against pre-stamp prod, so day 1.

**Definition of done.**

- All insert sites stamp the new columns. Coalescer + suppression rekeyed with dual-key transition live. Per-bot observability live. Per-(user, bot) pause read-path live (write-path deferred). Eval baseline captured. **No NOT NULL constraints yet.**
- Full pytest green, no regressions.
- Every new INSERT statement either includes `bot_id`/`topic_id` or is flagged by the lint (advisory).

## S1 lessons the executor must honor

1. **Before declaring any new script/CLI complete**: actually invoke it with `--help` (or equivalent entry point) to confirm it parses and basic argparse setup works. Pytest passing is necessary but not sufficient — pytest may bypass `main()`.
2. **Any SQL fragment in a Python string**: dry-run via `EXPLAIN` against the live schema, or sanity-check the aggregate/operator types against actual column types (e.g. `max(uuid)` doesn't exist in Postgres).
3. **Any asyncpg connection** against the configured `DATABASE_URL`: pass `statement_cache_size=0` — Supabase transaction-mode pooler (port 6543) is what's configured. Without this flag, scripts crash on first prepared-statement reuse.
4. **No `hash()` for deterministic IDs**: Python's `hash()` is salted per-process. Use `hashlib.sha1` for advisory-lock-id derivation or anything needing cross-run determinism.
5. **Schema filters in validation queries**: production tables live in schema `mediator`, NOT `public`. `information_schema.columns WHERE table_schema = 'mediator'`.

## Stop conditions

- Any test that was passing on `main` starts failing — STOP, report.
- Any work item's executor decides the design is wrong and tries to redesign — STOP, report.
- Schema migration would touch existing rows destructively — STOP, report.
- Any artifact INSERT path can't fit `artifact_topics` write in the same transaction — STOP, report.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: S2a — Stamp insert sites, [REDACTED], observability, eval baseline

## Overview

S1 landed the additive schema (topics, bots, dyads, bindings, channels, identities, `artifact_topics`, `topic_status`, `user_bot_state`, nullable scope columns on `messages`/`bot_turns`/`scheduled_jobs`/`feedback`/`bridge_candidates`/artifact tables) and skeleton helpers (`routing.py`, extended `TurnContext` + `BotSpec`). S2a is the write-path pass:

- Wire `routing.py` into the inbound flow so the bot/sender/binding are resolved per inbound payload.
- Stamp every relevant INSERT with `bot_id`/`topic_id` (and version stamps on `bot_turns`), and add an `artifact_topics` row in the **same statement** as every artifact INSERT (single-statement CTE = same transaction).
- Rekey the in-memory `BurstCoalescer` and the newer-inbound suppression queries with dual-key `(user_id)` + `(user_id, bot_id)` lookups, with `bot_id IS NULL` accepted in suppression during transition.
- Read-path consult `user_bot_state.paused` in addition to `system_state` for the mediator binding (write path stays deferred).
- Pipe `bot_id`/`topic_id`/`channel_id`/`binding_id` into every relevant `logger`/`record_turn_event` call.
- Capture an eval baseline snapshot under `tests/fixtures/eval_baseline/` against the **pre-stamp** code (this runs day 1, before the stamping changes land — or alternatively against the un-stamped code on the previous commit; see assumption below).
- Add an **advisory** linter (`scripts/lint_inserts.py`) that warns on INSERTs missing `bot_id`/`topic_id` and on artifact INSERTs not co-statemented with `artifact_topics`. CI surfaces output without failing.

Hard guardrails: do not edit the 9 pre-existing dirty files (they predate this sprint), do not modify `docs/multi-agent-architecture.md`, add **no** `NOT NULL` constraints, do not redesign coalescer/suppression interfaces (dual-key, not full rename), and never let an artifact INSERT live without an accompanying `artifact_topics` row in the same statement — STOP and report if any site doesn't fit.

S1 lessons to honor explicitly: invoke any new CLI with `--help` before declaring done; use `hashlib.sha1` for any deterministic IDs / `bot_spec_version` (`hash()` is process-salted); set `statement_cache_size=0` on any new asyncpg connection; use schema `mediator` filters when querying `information_schema`; sanity-check SQL fragments against actual column types.

Repo shape relevant to S2a (file:line):
- Inbound: `app/services/inbound.py:147-244` (`_insert_message` + `process_inbound`).
- Outbound: `app/services/messaging.py:42-93` (`_insert_outbound`) plus calls at `:171`, `:198`, `:233`, `:336`, `:341`, `:355`, `:382`, `:391`.
- Agentic turn open: `app/services/agentic.py:473-502` (`_open_turn` INSERTs `bot_turns`).
- Deferred turn job: `app/services/agentic.py:569-590` (`_defer_for_text_cap` INSERTs `scheduled_jobs`).
- Newer-inbound suppression: `app/services/agentic.py:592-625` (final-send) and `app/services/tools/read_tools.py:152-179` (incremental-send).
- Scheduler seeds: `app/services/scheduled_jobs.py:205-227` (heartbeat) and `app/services/scheduled_job_handlers.py:198-215, 306-334` (recurring scheduled-task + weekly summary), `app/services/checkins.py:37`.
- Scheduled-task writes: `app/services/tools/write_tools.py:1359-1382` (`schedule_task`).
- Artifact writes: `app/services/tools/write_tools.py:695-757` (memories), `:760-793` (themes), `:796-848` (watch_items), `:868-915` (observations), `:918-1102` (distillations / `add_distillation` + `revise_distillation`), `:1105-1133` (out_of_bounds).

## Phase 1: Wire routing into inbound and propagate scope to TurnContext

### Step 3: Wire `routing.py` into `process_inbound` (`app/services/inbound.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** a `_resolve_scope(pool, transport, address)` helper near `app/services/inbound.py:147` that calls `resolve_bot` → `resolve_sender` → `resolve_binding` and returns `(bot_id, topic_id, channel_id, binding_id)`. For the mediator-only case, `bot_id="mediator"` and `topic_id` = the `relationship` topic id (resolve once and cache on module load). Falls back to mediator defaults if any lookup misses (still mediator-only).
2. **Plumb** the resolved scope into `_insert_message` so the new columns `bot_id` / `topic_id` are stamped on the `messages` row (extend the INSERT in `inbound.py:161`). Pass `channel_id` too if the column is added — **NOTE:** no `channel_id` column exists on `messages` per `0023_nullable_scope_columns.sql`; ASSUMPTION: skip `channel_id` from `messages`, use it for observability only. Confirm before implementation; if a column is wanted, add a new migration `0025_messages_channel_id.sql` (still nullable).
3. **Pass** scope through to `coalescer.add(...)` so the burst can be keyed by `(user_id, bot_id)` (Step 8). Update the `add` signature to accept an optional `bot_id`.
4. **Stamp** the inbound feedback INSERT at `inbound.py:105` with `bot_id` and `topic_id` (mediator/relationship for now).

### Step 4: Surface scope on `TurnContext` in agentic open (`app/services/agentic.py`)

Compute `bot_spec_version` via `hashlib.sha1(repr(ctx.bot_spec).encode()).hexdigest()[:12]` (deterministic — S1 lesson #4 — never `hash()`). Compute `tool_schema_version` via `hashlib.sha1` over `tool_schemas.py` file bytes (read once at import). `hot_context_builder_version` stays the `BotSpec.hot_context_builder_version` literal.

## Phase 2: Stamp the remaining insert sites

**Scope:** Medium

2. **Stamp** the recurring `scheduled_task` reschedule at `scheduled_job_handlers.py:198-215` (`_schedule_next_recurrence` block) and the weekly-summary reschedule at `:316-334` with `bot_id`/`topic_id` from the job-row's existing `bot_id` (read-and-propagate). For S2a the row exists with `bot_id` already; fall back to mediator default if absent.

7. **Scheduler dispatcher**: change `ScheduledJobs.run_due` / `_dispatch` (in `scheduled_job_handlers.py`) to read the job's stored `bot_id` and pass it through to handlers (no-op for one bot, but the wiring is live per item 5 of §16.2).

### Step 7: Artifact writes + `artifact_topics` in same statement (`app/services/tools/write_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **For each** artifact INSERT (memories `:699`, supersede_memory new-row at `:746`, themes `:764`, watch_items `:800`, observations `:878`, distillations `:939` and `:1049`, out_of_bounds `:1109`), rewrite as a single-statement CTE:
   ```sql
   WITH new_artifact AS (
       INSERT INTO memories (..., recorded_by_bot_id)
       VALUES (..., $N)
       RETURNING id
   ),
   topic_link AS (
       INSERT INTO artifact_topics (artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id, tagged_by_bot_id, status)
       SELECT 'memories', new_artifact.id, $M, $N, 'active'
       FROM new_artifact
   )
   SELECT id FROM new_artifact
   ```
   Single statement = single implicit transaction (verified — Postgres CTEs run atomically). No `pool.acquire()` / `conn.transaction()` block is needed and we avoid the connection-shape churn.
2. **Stamp** `recorded_by_bot_id = ctx.bot_id` on each artifact INSERT, and use `ctx.primary_topic_id` for the matching `artifact_topics` row (one row per bot's primary topic — mediator's `relationship` for S2a).
3. **For `revise_distillation`** (`:1041-1092`, already a multi-CTE statement), extend the existing CTE with a third arm that inserts the `artifact_topics` row for the new distillation. Keep the existing `revised_old` arm.
4. **If any site cannot fit** the `artifact_topics` write inside the same statement (e.g. needs a follow-up scheduled job that depends on the new id), STOP and report. The `_schedule_context_job` calls for `watch_items` and `out_of_bounds` happen **after** the artifact INSERT row returns — those are fine (the artifact + its topic link are atomic; the scheduled-job side-effect is intentionally separate, same as today).

### Step 8: Feedback inserts (`app/services/discord.py`, `app/services/tools/write_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Stamp** the discord-side feedback INSERT at `discord.py:470` and the tool-side at `write_tools.py:1763` with `bot_id`/`topic_id` (mediator default for the bot, `ctx.primary_topic_id` for the tool-call site). The discord path doesn't have a `ctx` — pass `bot_id` from the resolved channel (Step 3 routing helper).

## Phase 3: Dual-key rekey + per-(user,bot) read paths

### Step 9: Dual-key BurstCoalescer (`app/services/debouncer.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Extend** `_Burst` to carry an optional `bot_id: str | None = None`.
2. **Change** internal dicts (`_bursts`, `_locks`, `_wait_tasks`, `_live_typing_tasks`, `_live_typing_stops`) so all lookups try `(user_id, bot_id)` first, then fall back to `(user_id,)` (legacy in-flight bursts from before the deploy). Keep the dict shape as a single dict keyed by a tuple `(user_id, bot_id_or_none)` — both keys coexist; lookups try both. Internal `AsyncBurstCoalescer` continues to be keyed by `user_id` since it's a third-party runtime and re-keying it is out of scope; the bot dispatch happens at our `_fire_batch` level.
3. **Add** `bot_id` kwarg to `add(...)` — default `None` so existing test call sites keep working. New inbound calls pass `bot_id` from Step 3.
4. **Drop the legacy reader path in S2b** — leave a `# TODO(S2b): drop legacy single-key fallback` comment at each fallback site.

### Step 10: Dual-key newer-inbound suppression (`app/services/agentic.py`, `app/services/tools/read_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Modify** `_newer_inbound_exists` at `agentic.py:592-625` to take `ctx` and add `AND (bot_id = $ctx.bot_id OR bot_id IS NULL)` to the EXISTS query. The `IS NULL` half catches legacy boundary rows written before the stamping deploy.
2. **Same** change at `read_tools.py:152-179` (`_newer_inbound_exists` for incremental send).
3. **No** call-site signature break — both functions already receive `ctx`/`user`.

### Step 11: Per-(user, bot) pause read path (`app/services/messaging.py`, `app/services/scheduled_job_handlers.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** a helper `await user_bot_paused(pool, user_id, bot_id) -> bool` in `app/services/system_state.py` reading `user_bot_state.paused`.
2. **Call** it alongside `system_state.is_paused(pool)` in:
   - `messaging.py:170` (`send_outbound_part`) and `:335` (`send_outbound`) — withhold if either is true.
   - Scheduler dispatch in `scheduled_job_handlers.py` before each user-facing job fires.
3. **Write path stays deferred**: no code writes to `user_bot_state.paused` in S2a; global pause via `system_state.pause` remains the kill switch. Document at the top of `system_state.py`.

## Phase 4: Observability + lint + tests

### Step 12: Per-bot observability fields on every log/event (`app/services/inbound.py`, `agentic.py`, `messaging.py`, `scheduled_job_handlers.py`, `oob_check.py`, `turn_audit.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

2. **Update** every `logger.info/.warning/.error` in inbound, agentic, messaging, scheduler, OOB to pass `extra=_obs_fields(ctx)` (or an equivalent shaped dict when no `TurnContext` is in scope — inbound builds it from the routing helper output). Don't churn the message templates; just add `extra=`.

### Step 13: Advisory linter for INSERTs (`scripts/lint_inserts.py`, CI workflow)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** `scripts/lint_inserts.py` that walks `app/` Python files and statically scans for SQL string literals matching `INSERT INTO (messages|bot_turns|scheduled_jobs|feedback)` — flag any that don't include both `bot_id` and `topic_id` in the column list.
2. **Same** scanner flags `INSERT INTO (memories|themes|observations|watch_items|distillations|out_of_bounds)` not followed (within the same SQL string) by `INSERT INTO artifact_topics`. Use a simple regex over the concatenated string literal of each call.
3. **CI step**: add a new `lint-inserts` job/step in the existing workflow that runs `python scripts/lint_inserts.py` with exit code 0 always in S2a (`|| true`) but prints findings — advisory only. Add a `# TODO(S2b): make blocking` comment at the script's exit-code line.
4. **Invoke** `python scripts/lint_inserts.py --help` to confirm argparse setup (S1 lesson #1). Output should be human-readable and machine-grep-able (`path:line: kind: missing column 'bot_id'`).

### Step 14: Tests / fixtures — accept `bot_spec` parameter (`tests/conftest.py` is dirty; use a sibling helper)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **DO NOT modify** `tests/conftest.py` (dirty file). Instead, add a small helper module `tests/_scope_helpers.py` that exposes `make_mediator_ctx(...)` building a `TurnContext` with mediator-default scope fields and a `bot_spec=` override knob.
2. **Update** new tests added in S2a (and any tests for stamping logic) to use the helper. Existing tests that construct `TurnContext` directly already pass; they need no change because the scope fields default to `None`.
3. **Add tests** in new files `tests/test_s2a_stamping.py`, `tests/test_s2a_coalescer_dual_key.py`, `tests/test_s2a_suppression_dual_key.py`, `tests/test_s2a_pause_read_path.py`, `tests/test_s2a_lint_inserts.py`:
   - Stamping tests assert that `messages`, `bot_turns`, `scheduled_jobs`, `feedback`, and every artifact write carries `bot_id` and `topic_id` (and `bot_spec_version` etc. on `bot_turns`), and that an `artifact_topics` row exists immediately after each artifact write (single-statement CTE → check by reading both back from the fake pool).
   - Coalescer tests assert that bursts keyed by `(user_id)` and `(user_id, bot_id)` flush correctly, and that during transition a flush for `(user_id, None)` doesn't drop a `(user_id, bot_id)` burst.
   - Suppression tests assert the EXISTS query filters by `(bot_id = ctx.bot_id OR bot_id IS NULL)`.
   - Pause read-path test toggles `user_bot_state.paused = TRUE` for the mediator binding and asserts `send_outbound` withholds even when `system_state.is_paused` is false.
   - Linter test invokes `scripts/lint_inserts.py` against a small fixture string and asserts the warning surface.

### Step 15: Run the suite, capture a post-stamp eval comparison, branch + checklist file
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** `pytest -q` — expect green. If any test passing on `main`/S1 fails, STOP and report (stop condition).
2. **Re-run** `python -m evals run` post-stamp and diff results against `tests/fixtures/eval_baseline/` — surface any drift in the PR description. No CI gate yet (eval baseline is a reference, not a regression test for S2a).
3. **Create** the feature branch tip (already on `s2a-stamp-rekey-observability`) and stage commits — exclude the 9 pre-existing dirty files. Do NOT commit `docs/multi-agent-architecture.md`.

## Execution Order

1. Phase 0 first — capture the eval baseline against pre-stamping code (Step 2 must run before any code changes; if already past that point, capture against `git stash` or `git worktree` of `d4c2a7c`).
2. Phase 1 (Steps 3–4) — routing + TurnContext + bot_turns stamping. Gates Phases 2–3.
3. Phase 2 (Steps 5–8) — stamp the remaining inserts. Artifact writes (Step 7) is the largest individual change and should land in its own commit.
4. Phase 3 (Steps 9–11) — dual-key coalescer, dual-key suppression, per-(user, bot) pause read. Each in a separate commit.
5. Phase 4 (Steps 12–15) — observability, linter, tests, final run.

## Validation Order

1. After Step 2: confirm `tests/fixtures/eval_baseline/` has one JSON per scenario and the README is locked. `python scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py --help` parses.
2. After Step 4: targeted `pytest tests/test_agentic.py tests/test_inbound_source.py tests/test_routing.py -q`.
3. After Step 7: `pytest tests/test_tools.py tests/test_s2a_stamping.py -q` — every artifact write should produce an `artifact_topics` row.
4. After Step 9: `pytest tests/test_debouncer.py tests/test_s2a_coalescer_dual_key.py -q`.
5. After Step 10: `pytest tests/test_agentic_lifecycle.py tests/test_s2a_suppression_dual_key.py -q`.
6. After Step 11: `pytest tests/test_send_outbound.py tests/test_pause_resume.py tests/test_s2a_pause_read_path.py -q`.
7. After Step 13: `python scripts/lint_inserts.py app/` — should warn (not fail) on any missed sites; iterate until output is empty for new code.
8. After Step 14: full `pytest -q` green.
9. Final: re-run `python -m evals run --prompt-version v1`, diff vs baseline.

  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-002",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan\u2019s `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, but the 0020 migration defines the table with a standard `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)` constraint on raw columns. postgresql `on conflict` target lists must reference the exact column names of a unique constraint or a matching unique index; expression constructs like `coalesce` in the target list do not match a raw-column unique constraint and will raise a syntax or 'no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification' error at runtime. the seed script will fail unless either (a) the table is changed to use an expression-based unique index with those exact coalesce expressions, or (b) the seed script is rewritten to query-first/insert-if-missing or uses the plain column names and accepts the null-handling semantics of the constraint.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-011",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase b cte pair (`ins` and `src`) requires identical where/order/limit clauses for cursor correctness. both are written aligned but nothing enforces the invariant during executor edits; drift would silently mis-advance cursor.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: routing unit tests require extending fakepool with `channels`, `user_identities`, `bot_bindings`, `dyads`, and `dyad_members` dicts so the sql patterns in `resolve_bot`, `resolve_sender`, and `resolve_binding` can match against stored rows. however, `tests/conftest.py` is explicitly listed in the brief as a dirty file that must not be touched. the revised plan says \"extend fakepool minimally\" twice (step 9 and step 11 assumption) but never specifies how to do so without modifying `conftest.py`\u2014for example by defining a local `routingfakepool(fakepool)` subclass inside `tests/test_routing.py` or by monkeypatching the class in that test module. if the executor follows the plan literally and edits `conftest.py`, they will violate a locked constraint. if they do not extend fakepool, the routing unit tests cannot be implemented as written. this is a supporting-infrastructure gap.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-003",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: new issue: the 0020 migration ddl defines `channels` with `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)`. postgresql treats null != null under standard unique constraints, so two whatsapp rows with `guild_id=null, channel_id=null` and the same transport+address would not conflict. however, `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, which requires a matching unique index on those coalesce expressions. without it, the seed script will raise 'there is no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification'. the ddl should either use `unique nulls not distinct` (pg15+, and the repo uses pg16 per `test_eval_workflow.py:36`) or a unique index on the coalesce expressions.",

    ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS topic_id uuid REFERENCES topics(id),
    ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS bot_id text REFERENCES bots(id);

-- ----- bot_turns -----
ALTER TABLE bot_turns
    ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS topic_id uuid REFERENCES topics(id),
    ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS bot_id text REFERENCES bots(id),
    ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS bot_spec_version text,
    ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS hot_context_builder_version text,
    ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS tool_schema_version text;

-- ----- scheduled_jobs -----

    ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS topic_id uuid REFERENCES topics(id),
    ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS bot_id text REFERENCES bots(id);

-- ----- feedback -----

    ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS topic_id uuid REFERENCES topics(id),
    ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS bot_id text REFERENCES bots(id);

-- ----- bridge_candidates -----

    ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS topic_id uuid REFERENCES topics(id),
    ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS bot_id text REFERENCES bots(id),
    ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS dyad_id uuid REFERENCES dyads(id);

-- ----- memories -----

-- ----- themes -----

    ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS recorded_by_bot_id text REFERENCES bots(id);

    ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS recorded_by_bot_id text REFERENCES bots(id);

    ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS recorded_by_bot_id text REFERENCES bots(id);

```
"""Routing helpers — resolve bots, senders, and bindings.

NOT wired into inbound.py yet. Sprint 1 delivers the module + unit tests.
Sprint 2a wires resolve_bot into the inbound path.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any, NamedTuple
from uuid import UUID


class BindingResolution(NamedTuple):
    binding_id: UUID
    bot_id: str
    dyad_id: UUID | None
    user_id: UUID | None


async def resolve_bot(pool: Any, *, transport: str, address: str) -> str | None:
    """Map a transport+address pair to a bot_id via the channels table.

    Returns the bot_id of the channel that matches, or None if no channel found.
    """
    row = await pool.fetchrow(
        """
        SELECT bot_id
        FROM channels
        WHERE transport = $1 AND address = $2
        LIMIT 1
        """,
        transport,
        address,
    )
    return row["bot_id"] if row is not None else None


async def resolve_sender(pool: Any, *, transport: str, address: str) -> UUID | None:
    """Map a transport+address pair to a user_id via user_identities.

    Returns the user_id, or None if the identity is not registered.
    """
    row = await pool.fetchrow(
        """
        SELECT user_id
        FROM user_identities
        WHERE transport = $1 AND address = $2
        """,
        transport,
        address,
    )
    return row["user_id"] if row is not None else None


async def resolve_binding(pool: Any, *, bot_id: str, user_id: UUID) -> BindingResolution | None:
    """Find the binding between a bot and a user.

    Looks up bot_bindings joined with dyad_members to find the binding
    that links this bot to this user (either directly via user_id or through
    dyad membership). Returns the BindingResolution or None.

    The ORDER BY makes the result deterministic if multiple bindings ever
    match — pick the oldest (earliest created_at) and break ties on id.
    """
    row = await pool.fetchrow(
        """
        SELECT bb.id    AS binding_id,
               bb.bot_id,
               bb.dyad_id,
               bb.user_id
        FROM bot_bindings bb
        LEFT JOIN dyad_members dm ON dm.dyad_id = bb.dyad_id
        WHERE bb.bot_id = $1
          AND (bb.user_id = $2 OR dm.user_id = $2)
        ORDER BY bb.created_at, bb.id
        LIMIT 1
        """,
        bot_id,
        user_id,
    )
    if row is None:
        return None
    return BindingResolution(
        binding_id=row["binding_id"],
        bot_id=row["bot_id"],
        dyad_id=row["dyad_id"],
        user_id=row["user_id"],
    )
```

tool_result Bash
```
BEGIN;

-- ============================================================
-- Sprint 1: artifact_topics — polymorphic join table linking artifacts to topics
-- ============================================================

-- Polymorphic join: (artifact_table, artifact_id) identifies the artifact row.
-- No FK to individual artifact tables — orphan prevention is application
-- discipline; lint catches it in S2a.
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS artifact_topics (
    artifact_table text NOT NULL,
    artifact_id uuid NOT NULL,
    topic_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES topics(id),
    status text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'active' CHECK (status IN ('active', 'retired')),
    tagged_by_bot_id text REFERENCES bots(id),
    reason text,
    created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    retired_at timestamptz,
    PRIMARY KEY (artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id)
);

-- ============================================================
-- Partial unique indexes — enforce at most one active row per (artifact, topic).
-- Retired rows coexist freely (historical record).
-- ============================================================

-- Prevent duplicate active assignments for the same (artifact_table, artifact_id)
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS artifact_topics_active_artifact_key
    ON artifact_topics (artifact_table, artifact_id)
    WHERE status = 'active';

-- Prevent multiple active rows for the same (artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id)
-- (covers the case where the same artifact is assigned to the same topic twice)
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS artifact_topics_active_unique_key
    ON artifact_topics (artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id)
    WHERE status = 'active';

-- ============================================================
-- Indexes
-- ============================================================
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_artifact_topics_topic_status
    ON artifact_topics (topic_id, status);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_artifact_topics_tagged_by
    ON artifact_topics (tagged_by_bot_id) WHERE tagged_by_bot_id IS NOT NULL;

-- ============================================================
-- RLS: deny anon
-- ============================================================
ALTER TABLE artifact_topics ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
ALTER TABLE artifact_topics FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
REVOKE ALL ON TABLE artifact_topics FROM anon;

DO $$
BEGIN
    CREATE POLICY deny_anon_artifact_topics ON artifact_topics
        FOR ALL TO anon USING (false) WITH CHECK (false);
EXCEPTION WHEN duplicate_object THEN NULL;
END $$;

COMMIT;BEGIN;

-- ============================================================
-- Sprint 1: topic_status — bot-authored summaries scoped to a topic+dyad or topic+user
--            user_bot_state — per-user, per-bot state (onboarding, paused)
-- ============================================================

-- 1. topic_status — each row captures a bot-authored status update scoped to
--    a topic and either a dyad or a single user (XOR constraint).
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS topic_status (
    id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    topic_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES topics(id),
    dyad_id uuid REFERENCES dyads(id),
    user_id uuid REFERENCES users(id),
    headline text NOT NULL,
    body text NOT NULL DEFAULT '',
    last_updated_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    last_updated_by_bot_id text NOT NULL REFERENCES bots(id),
    created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    CONSTRAINT topic_status_xor CHECK ((user_id IS NOT NULL) <> (dyad_id IS NOT NULL))
);
```

async def _open_turn(
    pool: Any,
    triggering_message_ids: list[UUID],
    user: User,
    prompt_snapshot: str,
    model_version: str,
    system_prompt_version: str,
) -> tuple[UUID, datetime]:
    row = await pool.fetchrow(
        """
        INSERT INTO bot_turns (
            triggered_by_message_id, triggering_message_ids, user_in_context,
            system_prompt_version, model_version, prompt_snapshot, prompt_snapshot_encrypted, started_at
        )
        VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5, $6, $7, now())
        RETURNING id, started_at
        """,
        triggering_message_ids[0] if triggering_message_ids else None,
        triggering_message_ids,
        user.id,
        system_prompt_version,
        model_version,
        prompt_snapshot,
        encrypt_value(prompt_snapshot),
    )
    try:
        started_at = row["started_at"]
    except KeyError:
        started_at = datetime.now(UTC)
    return row["id"], started_at


async def _complete_turn(

Bursts in-flight at deploy flush under old key; new bursts use the composite key. Drop the legacy reader in S2b.

**Definition of done.**

- All insert sites stamp the new columns. Coalescer + suppression rekeyed with dual-key transition live. Per-bot observability live. Per-(user, bot) pause read-path live (write-path deferred). Eval baseline captured. **No NOT NULL constraints yet.**
- Full pytest green, no regressions.
- Every new INSERT statement either includes `bot_id`/`topic_id` or is flagged by the lint (advisory).

## S1 lessons the executor must honor

1. **Before declaring any new script/CLI complete**: actually invoke it with `--help` (or equivalent entry point) to confirm it parses and basic argparse setup works. Pytest passing is necessary but not sufficient — pytest may bypass `main()`.
2. **Any SQL fragment in a Python string**: dry-run via `EXPLAIN` against the live schema, or sanity-check the aggregate/operator types against actual column types (e.g. `max(uuid)` doesn't exist in Postgres).
3. **Any asyncpg connection** against the configured `DATABASE_URL`: pass `statement_cache_size=0` — Supabase transaction-mode pooler (port 6543) is what's configured. Without this flag, scripts crash on first prepared-statement reuse.
4. **No `hash()` for deterministic IDs**: Python's `hash()` is salted per-process. Use `hashlib.sha1` for advisory-lock-id derivation or anything needing cross-run determinism.
5. **Schema filters in validation queries**: production tables live in schema `mediator`, NOT `public`. `information_schema.columns WHERE table_schema = 'mediator'`.

## Stop conditions

- Any test that was passing on `main` starts failing — STOP, report.
- Any work item's executor decides the design is wrong and tries to redesign — STOP, report.
- Schema migration would touch existing rows destructively — STOP, report.
- Any artifact INSERT path can't fit `artifact_topics` write in the same transaction — STOP, report.

        # Implementation Plan: S2a — Stamp insert sites, [REDACTED], observability, eval baseline

## Overview

S1 landed the additive schema (topics, bots, dyads, bindings, channels, identities, `artifact_topics`, `topic_status`, `user_bot_state`, nullable scope columns on `messages`/`bot_turns`/`scheduled_jobs`/`feedback`/`bridge_candidates`/artifact tables) and skeleton helpers (`routing.py`, extended `TurnContext` + `BotSpec`). S2a is the write-path pass.

**No root-cause re-think.** Every gate flag is a completeness/precision gap, not a wrong target. Revisions:

- **bridge_candidates** is now in scope for stamping (write_tools.py:305-329) and for the lint scanner table list (issue_hints-2).
- **Pause read-path survey** now explicitly covers `oob_check`, `withheld_reviews`, `recovery`, and `pacer` re-emission sites (scope flag).
- **write_tools.py edit safety** is spelled out: targeted `Edit` ops on the existing SQL string literals (column-list and VALUES insertions only), and the existing `supersede_memory` CTE at `:737-757` is **extended** with a new arm — never rewritten (issue_hints-4, correctness-4).
- **Async resolution** of the relationship topic id moves from "module-load" to a startup hook called from app init (mirrors `populate_mediator_spec_from_db`), with the cached value read from a module-level slot (FLAG-001, correctness-1).
- **Discord reaction handler** explicitly invokes `_resolve_scope` from the reaction code path (callers flag).
- **Identity-seeding pre-flight** check added before Step 3 (all_locations-1).
- **Eval baseline reproducibility**: capture via a `git worktree` pinned to `d4c2a7c`, not stash (all_locations-2).
- **bot_spec_version semantics**: doc note that the hash intentionally tracks every dataclass field — including incidental ones — so any BotSpec change rolls the version (correctness-3, settled by gate).
- **Settled decisions** (per gate, not relitigated): dual-key coalescer stays (FLAG-005/correctness-2), `channel_id` is observability-only on `messages` for S2a (issue_hints-1), lint advisory (already in plan).

Hard guardrails (unchanged): no edits to the 9 pre-existing dirty files except the targeted-INSERT-only carve-out for `write_tools.py`; no edits to `docs/multi-agent-architecture.md`; no NOT NULL constraints; no coalescer/suppression interface redesigns; STOP if any artifact INSERT can't fit `artifact_topics` in the same statement.

S1 lessons honored: `--help` smoke for every new CLI; `hashlib.sha1` (never `hash()`); `statement_cache_size=0` on new asyncpg connections; schema `mediator` for `information_schema` queries; SQL fragments sanity-checked against actual column types.

**Repo shape (file:line):**
- Inbound: `app/services/inbound.py:147-244` (`_insert_message` + `process_inbound`), feedback INSERT at `:103-113`.
- Outbound: `app/services/messaging.py:42-93` (`_insert_outbound`), send paths `:170`, `:335`.

## Phase 1: Wire routing into inbound; surface scope on TurnContext

### Step 3: Async startup hook for the relationship topic id (`app/bots/registry.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `populate_topic_ids_from_db(pool)` to `app/bots/registry.py`, mirroring `populate_mediator_spec_from_db`. Runs at app init, queries `SELECT id FROM mediator.topics WHERE slug = 'relationship'`, and stores the UUID on a module-level slot `_RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID` plus on the rebuilt `MEDIATOR_BOT` spec (extend `BotSpec` if needed — already has `primary_topic_slug`; we can also memoize a resolved-id field on the spec). Callers consume via a sync accessor `get_relationship_topic_id() -> UUID`.
2. **Wire** the call into `app/main.py` startup, immediately after `populate_mediator_spec_from_db`. Both run inside an event loop, so the async resolution is safe.
3. **Fallback** if the row is missing: log a loud warning and leave the slot `None`. Code that needs the id treats `None` as "no topic stamp"; tests inject a UUID via the helper from Step 14.

### Step 4: Wire `routing.py` into `process_inbound` (`app/services/inbound.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** `_resolve_scope(pool, transport, address) -> ResolvedScope` near `inbound.py:147`. Returns a tiny dataclass / NamedTuple of `(bot_id, topic_id, channel_id, binding_id)`. Internally calls `resolve_bot` → `resolve_sender` → `resolve_binding`. On any miss falls back to mediator defaults (`bot_id="mediator"`, `topic_id=get_relationship_topic_id()`, `channel_id=None`, `binding_id=None`).
2. **Plumb** the resolved scope into `_insert_message` so the `messages` INSERT at `inbound.py:161` stamps `bot_id` and `topic_id`. **`channel_id` stays observability-only for S2a** (no column on `messages` — gate-confirmed settled decision); the executor does not add a `channel_id` column or migration in S2a.
3. **Pass** scope to `coalescer.add(...)` so bursts are keyed `(user_id, bot_id)` (Step 10). `add` gains an optional `bot_id` kwarg, default `None`.
4. **Stamp** the inbound-feedback INSERT at `inbound.py:105` with `bot_id` and `topic_id` from the same resolved scope.

### Step 5: Surface scope on `TurnContext`; stamp `bot_turns` (`app/services/agentic.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Populate** `ctx.bot_id`, `ctx.bot_spec`, `ctx.binding_id`, `ctx.participants_shape`, `ctx.primary_topic_id`, `ctx.primary_topic_slug`, `ctx.channel_id` at every `TurnContext(...)` construction in `agentic.py`. Mediator-only defaults: `get_bot_spec("mediator")` + `get_relationship_topic_id()` from the slot populated in Step 3.
2. **Stamp** the `bot_turns` INSERT at `agentic.py:483` with `bot_id`, `topic_id`, `bot_spec_version`, `hot_context_builder_version`, `tool_schema_version`. Compute:
   - `bot_spec_version = hashlib.sha1(repr(ctx.bot_spec).encode()).hexdigest()[:12]`. **Intentional**: this hashes the full dataclass repr (including model strings, prompt versions, etc.), so any BotSpec field change rolls the version. That's the desired auditability — do not try to filter to "behavioral" fields.
   - `tool_schema_version`: precompute at import time as `hashlib.sha1(open(tool_schemas.__file__, 'rb').read()).hexdigest()[:12]`. `tool_schemas.py` is a dirty file but only its bytes are read — no edits.
   - `hot_context_builder_version`: read from `BotSpec.hot_context_builder_version` literal.
3. **Stamp** the deferred-turn `scheduled_jobs` INSERT at `agentic.py:577` with `bot_id` and `topic_id`.

## Phase 2: Stamp the remaining insert sites

**Scope:** Small
1. **Thread** `bot_id`/`topic_id` from `TurnContext` into `_insert_outbound` — both INSERTs at `messaging.py:55` and `:84` add the two columns.

### Step 8: Artifact writes + `artifact_topics` in same statement (`app/services/tools/write_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Large

> **Edit-safety contract for `write_tools.py`** (dirty file, INSERT-only carve-out): every change in this step is implemented via the `Edit` tool with a small `old_string` that uniquely matches the affected SQL fragment (column list, VALUES tuple, or CTE arm). **Do not** delete or re-flow surrounding code, comments, or whitespace. Specifically for the existing `supersede_memory` multi-CTE at `:737-757`, ADD a new CTE arm — do not rewrite the existing `WITH old AS (UPDATE memories ...)` block. Run `git diff --stat app/services/tools/write_tools.py` after each edit and confirm the changed-line count is roughly `+N` only.

1. **For each simple artifact INSERT** (memories `:699`, themes `:764`, watch_items `:800`, observations `:878`, distillations `:939`, out_of_bounds `:1109`), rewrite the single SQL string from:
   ```sql
   INSERT INTO memories (...) VALUES (...) RETURNING id
   ```
   to:
   ```sql
   WITH new_artifact AS (
       INSERT INTO memories (..., recorded_by_bot_id) VALUES (..., $K) RETURNING id
   ), topic_link AS (
       INSERT INTO artifact_topics (artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id, tagged_by_bot_id, status)
       SELECT 'memories', new_artifact.id, $M, $K, 'active' FROM new_artifact
   )
   SELECT id FROM new_artifact
   ```
   Postgres runs each statement atomically, so the artifact row + its `artifact_topics` row are guaranteed in the same transaction. No `pool.acquire()` block needed. `recorded_by_bot_id = ctx.bot_id`, `topic_id = ctx.primary_topic_id`.
2. **For `supersede_memory` (`:737-757`)** — already a multi-arm CTE (`WITH old AS UPDATE memories ... INSERT INTO memories SELECT ...`). **Extend**, do not rewrite: add a third arm `, topic_link AS (INSERT INTO artifact_topics ... SELECT 'memories', new.id, $M, $K, 'active' FROM new)` between the new-row INSERT and the trailing `RETURNING`. Adjust the trailing `RETURNING` to `SELECT id AS new_id, $1::uuid AS old_id FROM new` if needed. Keep the existing comments intact.
3. **For `revise_distillation` (`:1041-1092`)** — already a multi-arm CTE (`WITH old AS SELECT ..., new AS INSERT INTO distillations ..., revised_old AS UPDATE distillations ...`). **Add** a fourth arm `, topic_link AS (INSERT INTO artifact_topics ... SELECT 'distillations', new.id, $M, $K, 'active' FROM new)`. Keep `revised_old` and the existing `JOIN`/`SELECT` shape.
4. **Stop** and report if any artifact INSERT path needs a separate transaction (e.g. dependent on a returning value before the topic link can be computed). The `_schedule_context_job` follow-ups on `watch_items` / `out_of_bounds` are intentionally separate side-effects (artifact + topic link atomic; scheduled-job side-effect outside) and DO NOT trigger STOP.

### Step 9: Bridge candidate + feedback inserts (`app/services/tools/write_tools.py`, `app/services/discord.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Stamp** `bridge_candidates` INSERT at `write_tools.py:305-329` with `bot_id=ctx.bot_id`, `topic_id=ctx.primary_topic_id`, `dyad_id=ctx.binding_id` if `ctx.participants_shape == 'dyad'` (the column exists per `0023`; `binding_id` is the dyad's binding for mediator, so we look up `dyad_members` via `ctx.user.id` or store the dyad id separately on `TurnContext` if convenient). Same edit-safety contract as Step 8 — targeted Edit of the column list + VALUES tuple only.
2. **Stamp** feedback INSERTs:
   - `write_tools.py:1763` (`log_feedback` tool): `bot_id=ctx.bot_id`, `topic_id=ctx.primary_topic_id`.
   - `discord.py:470` (reaction-handler gateway path): **invoke `_resolve_scope(pool, 'discord', user_phone_or_id)`** from the reaction handler **before** the INSERT and pass the resolved `bot_id`/`topic_id`. The reaction handler currently does not call `process_inbound`; explicit `_resolve_scope` here is the wiring point (callers flag). Do not let the stamp fall through to `None`.

## Phase 3: Dual-key rekey + per-(user,bot) pause read paths (broader survey)

### Step 10: Dual-key BurstCoalescer (`app/services/debouncer.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Extend** `_Burst` with optional `bot_id: str | None = None`.
2. **Rekey** internal dicts (`_bursts`, `_locks`, `_wait_tasks`, `_live_typing_tasks`, `_live_typing_stops`) to use composite tuple keys `(user_id, bot_id_or_None)`. New inbound calls pass `bot_id`; legacy / test calls that don't pass `bot_id` use `(user_id, None)`. During the dual-key window we accept BOTH key shapes per locked decision #1 — lookup logic tries the composite first, falls back to `(user_id, None)`. Internal `AsyncBurstCoalescer` remains keyed by `user_id` (third-party; out of scope to re-key); the bot-aware dispatch happens at our `_fire_batch` boundary.
3. **Add** `bot_id` kwarg to `BurstCoalescer.add(...)` and propagate into `_Burst`.
4. **Leave a `# TODO(S2b): drop legacy (user_id, None) fallback`** at each fallback site for the S2b cleanup pass. (Gate confirmed dual-key is a locked decision — do not relitigate.)

### Step 11: Dual-key newer-inbound suppression (`app/services/agentic.py`, `app/services/tools/read_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Modify** `_newer_inbound_exists` at `agentic.py:592-625`: add `AND (bot_id = $4 OR bot_id IS NULL)` to the EXISTS query, bind `ctx.bot_id` as the fourth parameter. The `IS NULL` half catches legacy boundary rows from before the stamping deploy.
2. **Same** at `read_tools.py:152-179`.
3. Functions already accept `ctx`/`user`; no call-site signature break.

### Step 12: Per-(user, bot) pause read path — broader survey (`app/services/system_state.py`, `app/services/messaging.py`, `app/services/scheduled_job_handlers.py`, `app/services/oob_check.py`, `app/services/withheld_reviews.py`, `app/services/recovery.py`, `app/services/pacer.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** `await user_bot_paused(pool, user_id, bot_id) -> bool` in `app/services/system_state.py` (reads `user_bot_state.paused`).
2. **Survey** every user-facing emission / re-emission path and add the per-bot pause check:
   - `messaging.py:170` (`send_outbound_part`) and `:335` (`send_outbound`) — withhold if `is_paused OR user_bot_paused`.
   - `scheduled_job_handlers.py` dispatch (just before each user-facing handler fires).
   - `oob_check.py` — if any path here releases user-visible output, gate it.
   - `withheld_reviews.py` — release of withheld reviews; gate.
   - `recovery.py` — recovery-induced re-emissions; gate.
   - `pacer.py` — paced answer/reaction emissions; gate.
3. **Audit method**: for each file above, grep for calls into `send_outbound`, `send_outbound_part`, or direct `INSERT INTO messages ... 'outbound'` writes. Any site that emits user-facing output must pass through a pause check. **If any site does NOT need a check** (e.g. internal logging only), add a short comment `# pause-check N/A: <reason>` at that site so the audit is durable for S2b. STOP and report if a site is structurally hard to gate.
4. **Write path stays deferred**: no code writes `user_bot_state.paused = TRUE` in S2a. Global `/pause` remains the kill switch. Document at the top of `system_state.py`.

## Phase 4: Observability, lint, tests, validation

### Step 13: Per-bot observability fields (`app/services/turn_context.py`, then `inbound.py`, `agentic.py`, `messaging.py`, `scheduled_job_handlers.py`, `oob_check.py`, `turn_audit.py`, `discord.py`, `whatsapp.py`, `hooks.py`, `withheld_reviews.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** `obs_fields(ctx_or_scope) -> dict` to `app/services/turn_context.py` returning `{"bot_id": ..., "topic_id": ..., "channel_id": ..., "binding_id": ...}` (filtered to non-None entries).
2. **Update** `logger.info/.warning/.error` calls in the hot-path files (inbound, agentic, messaging, scheduler, OOB, turn_audit) to pass `extra=obs_fields(ctx)` — message templates unchanged, just add `extra=`.
3. **Extend** the same `extra=` wiring to the adjacent emitter files (`discord.py`, `whatsapp.py`, `hooks.py`, `withheld_reviews.py`). For sites where no `ctx` is available, construct a scope dict from the local `bot_id`/`topic_id`/`channel_id`/`binding_id` (or `_resolve_scope` result). If a site genuinely has no scope (purely transport-level error), skip it and add a one-line note in `migrations/validation/s2a_preflight.md`.
4. **Update** `record_turn_event` callers (`agentic.py`, `turn_audit.py`) so the `metadata` dict carries the four scope fields. The function signature already accepts `metadata`; keys-only change.
5. **Note** in `s2a_preflight.md`: dashboard panes for split-by-bot are downstream config, out of scope for this code change.

### Step 14: Advisory linter for INSERTs (`scripts/lint_inserts.py`, CI workflow)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** `scripts/lint_inserts.py` — argparse with `--help`. Walks `app/` Python files, finds SQL string literals matching `INSERT INTO (messages|bot_turns|scheduled_jobs|feedback|bridge_candidates)` and flags missing `bot_id`/`topic_id` (and `dyad_id` for `bridge_candidates`).
2. **Same** scanner flags `INSERT INTO (memories|themes|observations|watch_items|distillations|out_of_bounds)` whose enclosing SQL string does not also contain `INSERT INTO artifact_topics`.
3. **CI step**: add `lint-inserts` step in the existing CI workflow running `python scripts/lint_inserts.py || true` — exit code never fails the build (advisory). Add `# TODO(S2b): make blocking` comment in the script.
4. **Smoke**: `python scripts/lint_inserts.py --help`. Output format: `path:line: kind: missing column 'bot_id'`.

### Step 15: Tests + fixtures (`tests/_scope_helpers.py`, new `tests/test_s2a_*.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **DO NOT edit** the dirty `tests/conftest.py` / `tests/test_tools.py` / `tests/test_hot_context.py` / `tests/test_time_context.py`. Create `tests/_scope_helpers.py` exposing `make_mediator_ctx(...)` (builds a `TurnContext` with mediator-default scope) and `make_resolved_scope(...)` (mock `ResolvedScope`). Both accept a `bot_spec=` override.
2. **Add** tests in new files:
   - `tests/test_s2a_stamping.py` — `messages`/`bot_turns`/`scheduled_jobs`/`feedback`/`bridge_candidates` carry `bot_id`/`topic_id` (and `dyad_id` for bridge); `bot_turns` carries `bot_spec_version`/`hot_context_builder_version`/`tool_schema_version` (sha1, deterministic across runs).
   - `tests/test_s2a_artifact_topics_same_stmt.py` — every artifact write produces an `artifact_topics` row, asserted by reading both back from the fake pool after a single `fetchrow` call.
   - `tests/test_s2a_coalescer_dual_key.py` — bursts keyed `(user_id, bot_id)` and `(user_id, None)` flush correctly; no cross-key drop.
   - `tests/test_s2a_suppression_dual_key.py` — EXISTS query includes `(bot_id = $ OR bot_id IS NULL)`.
   - `tests/test_s2a_pause_read_path.py` — `user_bot_state.paused = TRUE` for the mediator binding withholds outbound even when `is_paused = FALSE`; covers messaging, scheduler dispatch, and at least one re-emission site (oob_check / withheld_reviews / recovery / pacer).
   - `tests/test_s2a_scheduler_dispatch_bot_id.py` — dispatcher reads `bot_id` from the row and propagates (assert via instrumented handler).
   - `tests/test_s2a_lint_inserts.py` — invokes `scripts/lint_inserts.py` against a fixture string, asserts warning surface.

### Step 16: Validate + branch hygiene
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** `pytest -q` — must be green. If any test passing on S1 fails, STOP and report.
2. **Re-run** `python -m evals run --prompt-version v1` post-stamp; compare against `tests/fixtures/eval_baseline/`. Note material drift in the PR description — not a CI gate in S2a.
3. **Verify** no commits touch the 9 dirty files outside the targeted-INSERT carve-out for `write_tools.py`. Run `git diff --stat <s1-tip>..HEAD -- <each dirty file>` to confirm `write_tools.py` shows only additive line-count changes co-located with INSERTs; the other 8 files show zero changes. Do NOT commit `docs/multi-agent-architecture.md`.

## Execution Order
1. **Phase 0** runs first — eval baseline captured in a `d4c2a7c` worktree before any code change.
2. **Phase 1** (Steps 3–5) — startup hook for topic id, routing wired into inbound, scope on `TurnContext`, `bot_turns` stamped. Gates Phases 2–3.
3. **Phase 2** (Steps 6–9) — remaining INSERT stamping. Step 8 (artifact CTEs) is the largest and lands in its own commit. Step 9 (bridge_candidates + feedback) closes the stamping pass.
4. **Phase 3** (Steps 10–12) — coalescer dual-key, suppression dual-key, pause read-path (incl. broader survey). One commit per step.
5. **Phase 4** (Steps 13–16) — observability fan-out, linter, tests, final run + branch hygiene.

## Validation Order
1. After Step 2: `tests/fixtures/eval_baseline/` populated; `python scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py --help` parses; worktree removed.
2. After Step 5: `pytest tests/test_agentic.py tests/test_inbound_source.py tests/test_routing.py -q`.
3. After Step 8: `pytest tests/test_s2a_stamping.py tests/test_s2a_artifact_topics_same_stmt.py -q` — every artifact write produces an `artifact_topics` row in the same statement.
4. After Step 9: targeted bridge / feedback tests in the new file.
5. After Step 10: `pytest tests/test_debouncer.py tests/test_s2a_coalescer_dual_key.py -q`.
6. After Step 11: `pytest tests/test_agentic_lifecycle.py tests/test_s2a_suppression_dual_key.py -q`.
7. After Step 12: `pytest tests/test_send_outbound.py tests/test_pause_resume.py tests/test_s2a_pause_read_path.py -q`.
8. After Step 14: `python scripts/lint_inserts.py app/` — iterate until output is empty for new code.
9. After Step 15: full `pytest -q` green.
10. Final: re-run `python -m evals run --prompt-version v1`, diff vs baseline; verify branch hygiene per Step 16.

        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-002",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan\u2019s `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, but the 0020 migration defines the table with a standard `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)` constraint on raw columns. postgresql `on conflict` target lists must reference the exact column names of a unique constraint or a matching unique index; expression constructs like `coalesce` in the target list do not match a raw-column unique constraint and will raise a syntax or 'no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification' error at runtime. the seed script will fail unless either (a) the table is changed to use an expression-based unique index with those exact coalesce expressions, or (b) the seed script is rewritten to query-first/insert-if-missing or uses the plain column names and accepts the null-handling semantics of the constraint.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-011",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase b cte pair (`ins` and `src`) requires identical where/order/limit clauses for cursor correctness. both are written aligned but nothing enforces the invariant during executor edits; drift would silently mis-advance cursor.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    }

      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: new issue: the 0020 migration ddl defines `channels` with `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)`. postgresql treats null != null under standard unique constraints, so two whatsapp rows with `guild_id=null, channel_id=null` and the same transport+address would not conflict. however, `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, which requires a matching unique index on those coalesce expressions. without it, the seed script will raise 'there is no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification'. the ddl should either use `unique nulls not distinct` (pg15+, and the repo uses pg16 per `test_eval_workflow.py:36`) or a unique index on the coalesce expressions.",

        Debt guidance:
        - These are known accepted limitations. Do not re-flag them unless the current plan makes them worse, broadens them, or fails to contain them.
        - Prefix every new concern with a subsystem tag followed by a colon, for example `Timeout recovery: retry backoff remains brittle`.
        - When a concern is recurring debt that still needs to be flagged, prefix it with `Recurring debt:` after the subsystem tag, for example `Timeout recovery: Recurring debt: retry backoff remains brittle`.



        Your output template is at: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Veas/.megaplan/plans/sprint-2a-stamp-rekey/critique_output.json
Read this file first — it contains 5 checks, each with a question and guidance.
For each check, investigate the codebase, then add your findings to the `findings` array for that check.

Each finding needs:
- "detail": what you specifically checked and what you found (at least a full sentence)
- "flagged": true if this describes a difference, risk, or tension — even if you think it's justified. false only if purely informational with no possible downside.
- Every check must end with at least one finding. Never leave a `findings` array empty. If you found no issue, add one detailed `flagged: false` finding explaining what you checked and why it appears clear.

When in doubt, flag it — the gate can accept tradeoffs, but it can't act on findings it never sees.

Good: {"detail": "Checked callers of nthroot_mod in solveset.py line 1205 — passes prime moduli only, consistent with the fix.", "flagged": false}
Good: {"detail": "The fix handles empty tuples but not single-element tuples which need a trailing comma.", "flagged": true}
Bad: {"detail": "No issue found", "flagged": false}  ← too brief, will be rejected
Bad: {"detail": "The hints suggest approach X but the plan uses Y. However Y is consistent with X's intent.", "flagged": false}  ← a different approach than the hints IS a flag. You found a divergence — flag it. The gate decides if it's acceptable.

After filling in checks, add any additional concerns to the `flags` array (e.g., security, performance, dependencies).
Use the standard format (id, concern, category, severity_hint, evidence). This array can be empty.

Workflow: read the file → investigate → read file again → add finding → write file back. Repeat for each check.

This is critique iteration 2. The template file includes prior findings with their status. Verify addressed flags were actually fixed, re-flag if inadequate, and check for new issues introduced by the revision.

        Additional guidelines:
        - Robustness level: standard. Use balanced judgment. Flag significant risks, but do not spend flags on minor polish or executor-obvious boilerplate.
        - Over-engineering: prefer the simplest approach that fully solves the problem.
        - Reuse existing flag IDs when the same concern is still open.
        - `verified_flag_ids`: list flag IDs from prior iterations that the revised plan actually resolves (e.g., if the plan was revised to fix FLAG-001, and you confirm the fix is correct, include "FLAG-001"). Only include flags you've verified — don't guess.
        - Verify that the plan follows the expected structure when validator warnings or the outline suggest drift.
        - Additional flags may use these categories: correctness, security, completeness, performance, maintainability, other.
        - Focus on concrete issues, not structural formatting.

    async def _handle_message_delete(self, message: dict[str, Any]) -> None:

    async def _handle_reaction_add(self, event: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
        user_id = str(event.get("user_id", ""))
        if not is_allowed_discord_user(user_id):
            return
        target_id = await self.pool.fetchval(
            "SELECT id FROM messages WHERE whatsapp_message_id=$1 AND direction='outbound'",
            str(event.get("message_id", "")),
        )
        if target_id is None:
            logger.info("ignoring discord reaction for unknown outbound message_id=%s", event.get("message_id"))
            return
        emoji = event.get("emoji", {}).get("name")

## S1 lessons the executor must honor

1. **Before declaring any new script/CLI complete**: actually invoke it with `--help` (or equivalent entry point) to confirm it parses and basic argparse setup works. Pytest passing is necessary but not sufficient — pytest may bypass `main()`.
2. **Any SQL fragment in a Python string**: dry-run via `EXPLAIN` against the live schema, or sanity-check the aggregate/operator types against actual column types (e.g. `max(uuid)` doesn't exist in Postgres).
3. **Any asyncpg connection** against the configured `DATABASE_URL`: pass `statement_cache_size=0` — Supabase transaction-mode pooler (port 6543) is what's configured. Without this flag, scripts crash on first prepared-statement reuse.
4. **No `hash()` for deterministic IDs**: Python's `hash()` is salted per-process. Use `hashlib.sha1` for advisory-lock-id derivation or anything needing cross-run determinism.
5. **Schema filters in validation queries**: production tables live in schema `mediator`, NOT `public`. `information_schema.columns WHERE table_schema = 'mediator'`.

## Stop conditions

- Any test that was passing on `main` starts failing — STOP, report.
- Any work item's executor decides the design is wrong and tries to redesign — STOP, report.
- Schema migration would touch existing rows destructively — STOP, report.
- Any artifact INSERT path can't fit `artifact_topics` write in the same transaction — STOP, report.

        # Implementation Plan: S2a — Stamp insert sites, [REDACTED], observability, eval baseline

## Overview

S1 landed the additive schema (topics, bots, dyads, bindings, channels, identities, `artifact_topics`, `topic_status`, `user_bot_state`, nullable scope columns on `messages`/`bot_turns`/`scheduled_jobs`/`feedback`/`bridge_candidates`/artifact tables) and skeleton helpers (`routing.py`, extended `TurnContext` + `BotSpec`). S2a is the write-path pass.

**No root-cause re-think — all open flags are correctness/precision gaps the executor can resolve with concrete wording.** The v2 → v3 deltas target only the open flags:

- **correctness-1 (frozen BotSpec)**: cache the relationship topic id in a **module-level slot only** (`app/bots/registry._RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID`). Do NOT mutate `MEDIATOR_BOT` and do NOT add a field to `BotSpec` (frozen dataclass; would also roll `bot_spec_version` on every deploy).
- **correctness-2 / FLAG-009 (discord reaction handler)**: `channels.address` for transport `'discord'` is the **bot's user id** (per `scripts/seed_channels.py:95-101`). The reaction handler must pass `settings.discord_bot_user_id` (decoded from `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` if unset, matching the S1 seeder) as the address, NOT the reacting user's id. Same shape for whatsapp: `phone_number_id`.
- **issue_hints (binding_id vs dyad_id)**: these are distinct columns. `bot_bindings.id` is the binding_id; `bot_bindings.dyad_id` is the dyad id. `routing.resolve_binding` already returns both. **Add a `dyad_id: UUID | None = None` field to `TurnContext`** (file is new from S1; not in the dirty list), populate it from `BindingResolution.dyad_id`, and stamp `bridge_candidates.dyad_id` from `ctx.dyad_id` — never from `ctx.binding_id`.
- **callers-1 (TurnContext sites)**: confirmed grep — only **two** app-side construction sites exist: `app/services/agentic.py:725` and `app/services/tools/consult_perspective.py:105`. Both must populate scope fields.
- **callers-2 (scheduler handler signatures)**: confirmed grep — every handler takes a single `job: dict[str, Any]` (`scheduled_job_handlers.py:78,98,112,134,145,149,160`). `bot_id`/`topic_id` arrive as `job["bot_id"]`/`job["topic_id"]` automatically once the dispatcher SELECT includes them. No kwarg surgery needed.
- **scope (gateway-side direct sends)**: confirmed grep — `discord.py` outbound emissions all route through `messaging.send_outbound` / `messaging.send_outbound_part`; `whatsapp.py` is the same. No direct `INSERT INTO messages ... 'outbound'` writes exist in gateway modules. Audit step records this so S2b doesn't re-survey.
- **all_locations-1 (capture script in worktree)**: write the capture script in the **main** working tree first, then `cp scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py /tmp/veas-s1-tip/scripts/` after the worktree is created. The S1 tip doesn't have the script; copying avoids committing to a throwaway branch.
- **all_locations-2 (gateway emitter line numbers)**: concrete sites enumerated — `discord.py:107-110` (`send_text`), `:115-122` (`add_reaction`), `:129-130` (`edit`), `:137-138` (`delete`), `:153-156` (`send_typing`), `:411-421` (gateway message handler), `:450-478` (reaction handler), `:481-522` (`catch_up_recent_messages`); `whatsapp.py` is a small file — apply `obs_fields` to its 4–6 log call sites.
- **all_locations-3 (FakePool CTE support)**: `tests/_scope_helpers.py` carries a small `StampingFakePool(FakePool)` subclass that pattern-matches the new CTEs (artifact + topic_link arms) and records both inserts in side tables so tests can assert both rows exist after a single `fetchrow`. No real DB required for S2a unit tests.

**Settled / not relitigated (per gate):**

- `bot_spec_version` = sha1(repr(BotSpec))[:12] intentionally tracks every dataclass field.

## Phase 1: Wire routing into inbound; surface scope on TurnContext

### Step 3: Async startup hook for the relationship topic id (`app/bots/registry.py`, `app/main.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `populate_topic_ids_from_db(pool)` to `app/bots/registry.py`. Queries `SELECT id FROM mediator.topics WHERE slug = 'relationship'` and assigns the UUID to a **module-level slot only**:
   ```python
   _RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID: UUID | None = None

   async def populate_topic_ids_from_db(pool):
       global _RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID
       row = await pool.fetchrow("SELECT id FROM mediator.topics WHERE slug = 'relationship'")
       _RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID = row["id"] if row else None

   def get_relationship_topic_id() -> UUID | None:
       return _RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID
   ```
   **Do NOT** mutate `MEDIATOR_BOT` or extend `BotSpec` — the dataclass is `frozen=True`, and adding a field would roll `bot_spec_version` on every deploy.
2. **Wire** the call into `app/main.py` startup, immediately after `populate_mediator_spec_from_db`. Both run inside the FastAPI lifespan event loop.
3. **Fallback** if the row is missing: log a loud warning; the slot stays `None`. Stamping callsites treat `None` as "no topic stamp" (column is nullable per `0023`). Tests inject a UUID via the helper from Step 15.

### Step 4: Extend `TurnContext` with `dyad_id`; wire `routing.py` into `process_inbound` (`app/services/turn_context.py`, `app/services/inbound.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** `dyad_id: UUID | None = None` to `TurnContext` (`app/services/turn_context.py:16-47`). File is **not** in the dirty list; safe to extend. `dyad_id` is distinct from `binding_id` — `BindingResolution` already exposes both.
2. **Add** `_resolve_scope(pool, transport, address) -> ResolvedScope` near `inbound.py:147`. Returns a NamedTuple `(bot_id, topic_id, channel_id, binding_id, dyad_id)`. Internally calls `resolve_bot` → `resolve_sender` → `resolve_binding`. On any miss falls back to mediator defaults (`bot_id="mediator"`, `topic_id=get_relationship_topic_id()`, others `None`).
3. **Plumb** the resolved scope into `_insert_message` so `inbound.py:161` stamps `bot_id` and `topic_id`. **`channel_id` stays observability-only** (settled decision — no `messages.channel_id` column added in S2a).
4. **Pass** scope to `coalescer.add(...)` so bursts are keyed `(user_id, bot_id)` (Step 10). `add(...)` gains an optional `bot_id` kwarg, default `None`.
5. **Stamp** the inbound-feedback INSERT at `inbound.py:105` with `bot_id` and `topic_id` from the same resolved scope.

### Step 5: Surface scope on every TurnContext construction; stamp `bot_turns` (`app/services/agentic.py`, `app/services/tools/consult_perspective.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Enumerated TurnContext construction sites in `app/`** (grep-confirmed):
   - `app/services/agentic.py:725` (main turn open).
   - `app/services/tools/consult_perspective.py:105` (consult sub-turn).
   Both must populate `bot_id`, `bot_spec`, `binding_id`, `dyad_id`, `participants_shape`, `primary_topic_id`, `primary_topic_slug`, `channel_id`. Mediator-only defaults: `get_bot_spec("mediator")` + `get_relationship_topic_id()`. The consult sub-turn inherits scope from its parent `ctx` — copy fields verbatim, do not re-resolve.
2. **Stamp** the `bot_turns` INSERT at `agentic.py:483` with:
   - `bot_id`, `topic_id` from `ctx`.
   - `bot_spec_version = hashlib.sha1(repr(ctx.bot_spec).encode()).hexdigest()[:12]` — **intentionally** hashes every dataclass field; rolls on any change.
   - `tool_schema_version`: at import time, `hashlib.sha1(open(tool_schemas.__file__, 'rb').read()).hexdigest()[:12]`. Reads `tool_schemas.py` bytes only — no edits to the dirty file.
   - `hot_context_builder_version`: `ctx.bot_spec.hot_context_builder_version`.
3. **Stamp** the deferred-turn `scheduled_jobs` INSERT at `agentic.py:577` with `bot_id` and `topic_id`.
