### Step 8: Artifact writes + `artifact_topics` in same statement (`app/services/tools/write_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Large

> **Edit-safety contract for `write_tools.py`** (dirty file, INSERT-only carve-out):
> - Every change uses the `Edit` tool with a small unique `old_string` matching only the affected SQL fragment (column list, VALUES tuple, or one CTE arm).
> - **Do not** delete or re-flow surrounding code, comments, or whitespace.
> - **Each `write_tools.py` change lands in its own commit** so accidental reflow is caught at review time.
> - After every edit, run `git diff --stat app/services/tools/write_tools.py` and confirm only additive changes appear at the targeted line range.
> - For existing multi-arm CTEs (`supersede_memory`, `revise_distillation`), **ADD a new arm** — never rewrite.

1. **For each simple artifact INSERT** (memories `:699`, themes `:764`, watch_items `:800`, observations `:878`, distillations `:939`, out_of_bounds `:1109`), rewrite the SQL string from:
   ```sql
   INSERT INTO memories (...) VALUES (...) RETURNING id
   ```
   to:
   ```sql
   WITH new_artifact AS (
       INSERT INTO memories (..., recorded_by_bot_id) VALUES (..., $K) RETURNING id
   ), topic_link AS (
       INSERT INTO artifact_topics (artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id, tagged_by_bot_id, status)
       SELECT 'memories', new_artifact.id, $M, $K, 'active' FROM new_artifact
   )
   SELECT id FROM new_artifact
   ```
   Postgres runs each statement atomically. `recorded_by_bot_id = ctx.bot_id`, `topic_id = ctx.primary_topic_id`.
2. **`supersede_memory` (`:737-757`)** — existing multi-arm CTE. **ADD** a third arm between the inner `INSERT INTO memories ... RETURNING` and the trailing `RETURNING`:
   ```sql
   , topic_link AS (
       INSERT INTO artifact_topics (artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id, tagged_by_bot_id, status)
       SELECT 'memories', new.id, $M, $K, 'active' FROM new
   )
   ```
   Keep `WITH old AS (UPDATE memories ...)` verbatim.
3. **`revise_distillation` (`:1041-1092`)** — existing multi-arm CTE (`old`, `new`, `revised_old`). **ADD** a fourth arm:
   ```sql
   , topic_link AS (
       INSERT INTO artifact_topics (artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id, tagged_by_bot_id, status)
       SELECT 'distillations', new.id, $M, $K, 'active' FROM new
   )
   ```
   Keep `revised_old` and the trailing `JOIN`/`SELECT` shape.
4. **STOP** and report if any artifact INSERT can't fit `artifact_topics` in the same statement. The `_schedule_context_job` follow-ups on `watch_items`/`out_of_bounds` are intentionally separate side-effects (artifact + topic link atomic; scheduled-job follow-up outside) and DO NOT trigger STOP.

### Step 9: Bridge candidate + feedback inserts (`app/services/tools/write_tools.py`, `app/services/discord.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Stamp** `bridge_candidates` INSERT at `write_tools.py:305-329`:
   - `bot_id = ctx.bot_id`
   - `topic_id = ctx.primary_topic_id`
   - `dyad_id = ctx.dyad_id` (NOT `ctx.binding_id` — they are distinct columns: `bot_bindings.id` vs `bot_bindings.dyad_id`). `ctx.dyad_id` is populated by `_resolve_scope` from `BindingResolution.dyad_id` per Step 4.1.
   Use the Step 8 edit-safety contract (targeted Edit on the column list + VALUES tuple only).
2. **Stamp** feedback INSERTs:
   - `write_tools.py:1763` (`log_feedback`): `bot_id=ctx.bot_id`, `topic_id=ctx.primary_topic_id`.
   - `discord.py:468-478` reaction handler: invoke `_resolve_scope` with the **bot's** address (matches `channels.address` per `scripts/seed_channels.py:95-101`):
     ```python
     bot_user_id = get_settings().discord_bot_user_id  # same env var the S1 seeder reads
     scope = await _resolve_scope(self.pool, 'discord', bot_user_id)
     ```
     If `discord_bot_user_id` is unset, fall back to decoding it from `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` the same way `scripts/seed_channels.py:_decode_discord_user_id` does. Pass the resolved `scope.bot_id`/`scope.topic_id` into the feedback INSERT — never the reacting user's id.

## Phase 3: Dual-key rekey + per-(user,bot) pause read paths (broader survey)

### Step 10: Dual-key BurstCoalescer (`app/services/debouncer.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Extend** `_Burst` with optional `bot_id: str | None = None`.
2. **Rekey** internal dicts to composite tuple keys `(user_id, bot_id_or_None)`. New inbound passes `bot_id`; legacy/test calls without `bot_id` land on `(user_id, None)`. Lookups try the composite first, fall back to `(user_id, None)` (locked decision #1). Internal `AsyncBurstCoalescer` (third-party) remains keyed by `user_id`; bot-aware dispatch happens at our `_fire_batch` boundary.
3. **Add** `bot_id` kwarg to `BurstCoalescer.add(...)` (default `None`) and propagate into `_Burst`.
4. **Leave `# TODO(S2b): drop legacy (user_id, None) fallback`** at each fallback site.

### Step 11: Dual-key newer-inbound suppression (`app/services/agentic.py`, `app/services/tools/read_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Modify** `_newer_inbound_exists` at `agentic.py:592-625`: add `AND (bot_id = $4 OR bot_id IS NULL)` to the EXISTS query, bind `ctx.bot_id` as a new parameter.
2. **Same** at `read_tools.py:152-179`.

### Step 12: Per-(user, bot) pause read path — broader survey + gateway audit (`app/services/system_state.py`, `messaging.py`, `scheduled_job_handlers.py`, `oob_check.py`, `withheld_reviews.py`, `recovery.py`, `pacer.py`, `discord.py`, `whatsapp.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** `user_bot_paused(pool, user_id, bot_id) -> bool` to `system_state.py` (reads `user_bot_state.paused`).
2. **Gate** every user-facing emission/re-emission path. Sites:
   - `messaging.py:170` (`send_outbound_part`), `:335` (`send_outbound`).
   - `scheduled_job_handlers.py` dispatch before each user-facing handler fires.
   - `oob_check.py` — user-visible release.
   - `withheld_reviews.py` — released-review emissions.
   - `recovery.py:86,114` — recovery re-injects via `coalescer.add`/`add_burst`; the resulting turn goes through `send_outbound`, which gates centrally. Note `# pause-check via send_outbound` at those sites — no extra check needed.
   - `pacer.py` — paced answer/reaction emissions.
   - `discord.py`/`whatsapp.py` gateway sends: **grep-audited** in Step 1.4. All outbound emissions route through `messaging.send_outbound`/`send_outbound_part`. If any direct `INSERT INTO messages ... 'outbound'` is found in a gateway module, add the gate there too; otherwise document `# pause-check N/A: routes through send_outbound`.
3. **Audit method** for each site: grep for `send_outbound`, `send_outbound_part`, or direct outbound INSERTs. Each must either pass through a pause check OR carry a `# pause-check N/A: <reason>` comment. STOP and report if a site is structurally hard to gate.
4. **Write path stays deferred**: no code writes to `user_bot_state.paused` in S2a. Global `/pause` remains the kill switch. Document at the top of `system_state.py`.

## Phase 4: Observability, lint, tests, validation

### Step 13: Per-bot observability fields (`app/services/turn_context.py`, then enumerated emitter files)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** `obs_fields(ctx_or_scope) -> dict` to `app/services/turn_context.py` returning `{"bot_id": ..., "topic_id": ..., "channel_id": ..., "binding_id": ...}` (filter out None).
2. **Update** hot-path loggers to pass `extra=obs_fields(ctx)` — message templates unchanged:
   - `app/services/inbound.py` (all `logger.warning/.info`).
   - `app/services/agentic.py` (all `logger.*`).
   - `app/services/messaging.py` (all `logger.*`).
   - `app/services/scheduled_job_handlers.py` (all `logger.*`).
   - `app/services/oob_check.py` (all `logger.*`).
   - `app/services/turn_audit.py` (event metadata; see step 4).
3. **Update** adjacent emitters with **concrete line ranges**:
   - `app/services/discord.py:107-110` (`send_text`), `:115-122` (`add_reaction`), `:129-130` (edit), `:137-138` (delete), `:153-156` (`send_typing`), `:411-421` (gateway message handler), `:450-478` (reaction handler), `:481-522` (`catch_up_recent_messages`). For sites with no `ctx`, build a scope dict from local `bot_id`/`channel_id` or call `_resolve_scope` once at top of the function.
   - `app/services/whatsapp.py`: all `logger.*` (small file — apply to every site).
   - `app/services/hooks.py`: all `logger.*` invocations carry scope when one is in flight.
   - `app/services/withheld_reviews.py`: all `logger.*` plus the row INSERTs (the function already takes structural args; thread `bot_id`/`topic_id` from the caller).
   For sites with **no scope context** (purely transport-level connection errors), skip and add a one-line `# obs N/A: transport-only` comment.
4. **Update** `record_turn_event` callers (`agentic.py`, `turn_audit.py`) so the `metadata` dict carries `bot_id`/`topic_id`/`channel_id`/`binding_id`. Function shape unchanged.
5. **Note** in `s2a_preflight.md`: dashboard panes (split-by-bot) are downstream config, out of scope.

### Step 14: Advisory linter for INSERTs (`scripts/lint_inserts.py`, CI workflow)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** `scripts/lint_inserts.py` — argparse with `--help`. Walks `app/` Python files; finds SQL string literals matching `INSERT INTO (messages|bot_turns|scheduled_jobs|feedback|bridge_candidates)` and flags missing `bot_id`/`topic_id` (and `dyad_id` for `bridge_candidates`).
2. **Same** scanner flags `INSERT INTO (memories|themes|observations|watch_items|distillations|out_of_bounds)` whose enclosing SQL string lacks an `INSERT INTO artifact_topics` clause.
3. **CI step**: `lint-inserts` runs `python scripts/lint_inserts.py || true` — exit always 0 in S2a. `# TODO(S2b): make blocking` comment in the script.
4. **Smoke** `python scripts/lint_inserts.py --help`. Output format: `path:line: kind: missing column 'bot_id'`.

### Step 15: Tests + fixtures (`tests/_scope_helpers.py`, new `tests/test_s2a_*.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Do NOT edit** dirty test files (`tests/conftest.py`, `tests/test_tools.py`, `tests/test_hot_context.py`, `tests/test_time_context.py`). Create `tests/_scope_helpers.py` exposing:
   - `make_mediator_ctx(bot_spec=None, **overrides)` building a `TurnContext` with mediator defaults (including `dyad_id`).
   - `make_resolved_scope(**overrides)` mock `ResolvedScope`.
   - **`StampingFakePool(FakePool)`** subclass (extends the `FakePool` exposed by `tests/conftest.py`) that pattern-matches the new artifact-CTE shape: when it sees `WITH new_artifact AS (INSERT INTO <table> ...), topic_link AS (INSERT INTO artifact_topics ...)`, it records both rows in `_artifacts` and `_artifact_topics` side dicts. Tests assert both rows exist after a single `fetchrow`. No real DB.
2. **Add** new test files (do not extend dirty files):
   - `tests/test_s2a_stamping.py` — verifies `messages`/`bot_turns`/`scheduled_jobs`/`feedback`/`bridge_candidates` stamps incl. `bot_spec_version`/`hot_context_builder_version`/`tool_schema_version` (sha1, deterministic across two process runs) and `bridge_candidates.dyad_id`.
   - `tests/test_s2a_artifact_topics_same_stmt.py` — every artifact write produces an `artifact_topics` row via `StampingFakePool`.
   - `tests/test_s2a_coalescer_dual_key.py` — `(user_id, bot_id)` and `(user_id, None)` keys flush correctly.
   - `tests/test_s2a_suppression_dual_key.py` — EXISTS includes `(bot_id = $ OR bot_id IS NULL)`.
   - `tests/test_s2a_pause_read_path.py` — `user_bot_state.paused=TRUE` withholds at messaging, scheduler dispatch, and at least one re-emission site (withheld_reviews or pacer).
   - `tests/test_s2a_scheduler_dispatch_bot_id.py` — instrumented handler asserts `job["bot_id"]` is populated.
   - `tests/test_s2a_lint_inserts.py` — invokes `scripts/lint_inserts.py` against fixture strings.

### Step 16: Validate + branch hygiene
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** `pytest -q` — must be green. If any S1-passing test fails, STOP.
2. **Re-run** `python -m evals run --prompt-version v1` post-stamp; diff against `tests/fixtures/eval_baseline/`. Note drift in the PR description.
3. **Verify** branch hygiene: `git diff --stat <s1-tip>..HEAD -- <each dirty file>`. The 8 non-`write_tools.py` dirty files show zero changes; `write_tools.py` shows only additive INSERT-co-located changes. `docs/multi-agent-architecture.md` not committed.
4. **Verify** each `write_tools.py` commit is independently atomic (per the edit-safety contract in Step 8).

## Execution Order
1. **Phase 0** first — eval baseline in the `d4c2a7c` worktree before any code change.
2. **Phase 1** (Steps 3–5) — startup hook, `TurnContext.dyad_id`, routing wired into inbound, scope on every `TurnContext` site, `bot_turns` stamped.
3. **Phase 2** (Steps 6–9) — remaining INSERT stamping. Step 8 lands one commit per artifact CTE rewrite/extend.
4. **Phase 3** (Steps 10–12) — coalescer dual-key, suppression dual-key, pause read-path with broader survey + gateway audit.
5. **Phase 4** (Steps 13–16) — observability fan-out, linter, tests, final run + branch hygiene.

## Validation Order
1. After Step 2: `tests/fixtures/eval_baseline/` populated; worktree removed; `python scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py --help` parses.
2. After Step 5: `pytest tests/test_agentic.py tests/test_inbound_source.py tests/test_routing.py -q`.
3. After Step 8: `pytest tests/test_s2a_stamping.py tests/test_s2a_artifact_topics_same_stmt.py -q`.
4. After Step 9: bridge / feedback tests in the new file pass.
5. After Step 10: `pytest tests/test_debouncer.py tests/test_s2a_coalescer_dual_key.py -q`.
6. After Step 11: `pytest tests/test_agentic_lifecycle.py tests/test_s2a_suppression_dual_key.py -q`.
7. After Step 12: `pytest tests/test_send_outbound.py tests/test_pause_resume.py tests/test_s2a_pause_read_path.py -q`.
8. After Step 14: `python scripts/lint_inserts.py app/` — iterate until empty for new code.
9. After Step 15: full `pytest -q` green.
10. Final: `python -m evals run --prompt-version v1` diff vs baseline; Step 16 branch hygiene verified.

        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-002",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan\u2019s `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, but the 0020 migration defines the table with a standard `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)` constraint on raw columns. postgresql `on conflict` target lists must reference the exact column names of a unique constraint or a matching unique index; expression constructs like `coalesce` in the target list do not match a raw-column unique constraint and will raise a syntax or 'no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification' error at runtime. the seed script will fail unless either (a) the table is changed to use an expression-based unique index with those exact coalesce expressions, or (b) the seed script is rewritten to query-first/insert-if-missing or uses the plain column names and accepts the null-handling semantics of the constraint.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-011",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase b cte pair (`ins` and `src`) requires identical where/order/limit clauses for cursor correctness. both are written aligned but nothing enforces the invariant during executor edits; drift would silently mis-advance cursor.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-001",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the scripts/seed_channels.py plan uses on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing, but the 0020 migration creates a plain unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id) constraint. postgresql requires the on conflict target to match a unique constraint or index exactly; an expression-based target without a matching expression index will raise a runtime error. additionally, the raw unique constraint treats nulls as distinct, so it cannot deduplicate whatsapp channels where guild_id and channel_id are null. the executor must either add a matching unique expression index in 0020, or rewrite the seed script to use a pre-insert select check instead of on conflict.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-004",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the 0020 migration plan for user_identities omits the verified_at timestamptz column present in architecture doc \u00a712.2 ddl, while claiming 'exactly per \u00a73/\u00a712 ddl'. since account-linking workflows are deferred beyond s1, the column is not functionally load-bearing yet, but the claim of exact ddl match is incorrect.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-009",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the resolve_binding sql in app/services/routing.py uses limit 1 without order by. if a user ever has both a solo binding and a dyad binding to the same bot, the returned row is non-deterministic. the schema has no unique constraint preventing multiple bindings per (bot_id, user_id). since s1 only seeds one dyad binding and the module is not wired into inbound yet, this is latent future risk rather than an immediate s1 blocker.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: user notes #1/#2 asked to restructure user_actions.md (split t14, move u1 to after execute). the plan addresses the spirit via execution order but does not enumerate user_actions.md content changes. if the harness consumes a separate user_actions.md, it may still reflect pre-revision ordering.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-008",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: routing unit tests require extending fakepool with `channels`, `user_identities`, `bot_bindings`, `dyads`, and `dyad_members` dicts so the sql patterns in `resolve_bot`, `resolve_sender`, and `resolve_binding` can match against stored rows. however, `tests/conftest.py` is explicitly listed in the brief as a dirty file that must not be touched. the revised plan says \"extend fakepool minimally\" twice (step 9 and step 11 assumption) but never specifies how to do so without modifying `conftest.py`\u2014for example by defining a local `routingfakepool(fakepool)` subclass inside `tests/test_routing.py` or by monkeypatching the class in that test module. if the executor follows the plan literally and edits `conftest.py`, they will violate a locked constraint. if they do not extend fakepool, the routing unit tests cannot be implemented as written. this is a supporting-infrastructure gap.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [

async def close_client() -> None:
    global _client
    if _client is not None:
        await _client.aclose()
        _client = None


async def _get_client() -> httpx.AsyncClient:
    if _client is None:
        return await init_client()
    return _client


async def send_text(to: str, body: str, *, send_typing_indicator: bool = True) -> dict[str, Any]:

    rest = await _rest_client()
    channel_id = await get_dm_channel_id(_discord_user_id(to))
    if send_typing_indicator:
        await send_typing(channel_id)
    message_response = await rest.send_message(channel_id, content=body)

## Orchestration

S1's files (everything new since branch `s1-foundation`) are on disk and committed — build on top of them.

**Locked decisions (do not relitigate during plan/critique):**
1. **Dual-key transition stays dual.** Coalescer and newer-inbound suppression service BOTH `(user_id)` and `(user_id, bot_id)` keys for the duration of S2a. Legacy reader paths are retired in S2b, not here.

## Sprint 2a — §16.2 verbatim

**Goal.** Insert sites write the new columns; coalescer and newer-inbound suppression are rekeyed with a dual-key transition; per-bot observability comes online; pre-S3 eval baseline captured. Mediator behavior unchanged. **No NOT NULL.**

**Pre-flight context.**

- Confirm S1 backfill held — query `SELECT count(*) FROM mediator.artifact_topics` (must be 235 from S1; no new writes yet).
- Exhaustive grep sweep of insert sites, broader than the obvious ones: `inbound.py:161`, `messaging.py:55, 84`, `agentic.py:483`, `agentic.py:577` (deferred jobs), `scheduled_jobs.py:216` (scheduler seeds), `write_tools.py:1367` (scheduled-task writes), every `INSERT INTO memories|themes|observations|watch_items|distillations|out_of_bounds` site. Checklist file local to the sprint.

**Work items, ordered.**

1. Wire `routing.py` into `inbound.py:23-180`. Populate `TurnContext` with real scope fields (still mediator-only defaults).
2. Stamp insert sites:
   - `inbound.py:161` — `bot_id`, `topic_id`, `channel_id`.
   - `messaging.py:55, 84` — same on outbound.
   - `agentic.py:483` — `bot_id`, `topic_id`, `bot_spec_version` (hash of `BotSpec` repr), `hot_context_builder_version`, `tool_schema_version` (hash of `tool_schemas.py`).
   - `agentic.py:577` deferred jobs and `scheduled_jobs.py:216` scheduler seeds — stamp `bot_id`, `topic_id`.
   - `write_tools.py:1367` scheduled-task writes — same.
   - Every artifact write in `write_tools.py`: stamp `recorded_by_bot_id` AND create matching `artifact_topics` row(s) for the bot's primary topic in the same transaction.
3. **Dual-key burst coalescer** (`app/services/debouncer.py`). In-memory dicts keyed by `user_id` today; new code services BOTH `(user_id)` and `(user_id, bot_id)` keys during this sprint. Bursts in-flight at deploy flush under old key; new bursts use the composite key. Drop the legacy reader in S2b.
4. **Dual-key newer-inbound suppression**:
   - `agentic.py:612` (final-send suppression).
   - `read_tools.py:166` (incremental-send suppression).
   - Filter: `(bot_id = ctx.bot_id OR bot_id IS NULL)` during transition — the `IS NULL` half catches legacy boundary rows written before the stamping deploy.
5. Scheduled-job dispatcher (`scheduled_jobs.py`) reads `bot_id` from row. No-op with one bot but the code path is live.
6. **Per-bot observability**: every log line and structured event in inbound, agentic, messaging, scheduler, OOB check carries `bot_id`, `topic_id`, `channel_id`, `binding_id`. Dashboards split by bot (even though only one bot exists yet — the panes are there for S5).
7. **Per-(user, bot) pause read path**: outbound and scheduler consult `user_bot_state.paused` in addition to `system_state.is_paused`. Write path for per-(user, bot) pause stays deferred. Global pause remains the kill switch.
8. **Eval baseline**: run the existing mediator eval suite against current prod-like state and snapshot results. This is the pre-S3 regression reference.
9. Tests/fixtures take a `bot_spec` argument (default mediator).
10. **Lint**: CI warns (does NOT fail) on any new artifact INSERT outside a transaction that also creates the `artifact_topics` row, and on any new `INSERT INTO messages|bot_turns|scheduled_jobs|feedback` missing `bot_id`/`topic_id`.

Dependencies: (1) gates (2–7); (8) runs against pre-stamp prod, so day 1.

**Definition of done.**

- All insert sites stamp the new columns. Coalescer + suppression rekeyed with dual-key transition live. Per-bot observability live. Per-(user, bot) pause read-path live (write-path deferred). Eval baseline captured. **No NOT NULL constraints yet.**
- Full pytest green, no regressions.
- Every new INSERT statement either includes `bot_id`/`topic_id` or is flagged by the lint (advisory).

## S1 lessons the executor must honor

1. **Before declaring any new script/CLI complete**: actually invoke it with `--help` (or equivalent entry point) to confirm it parses and basic argparse setup works. Pytest passing is necessary but not sufficient — pytest may bypass `main()`.
2. **Any SQL fragment in a Python string**: dry-run via `EXPLAIN` against the live schema, or sanity-check the aggregate/operator types against actual column types (e.g. `max(uuid)` doesn't exist in Postgres).
3. **Any asyncpg connection** against the configured `DATABASE_URL`: pass `statement_cache_size=0` — Supabase transaction-mode pooler (port 6543) is what's configured. Without this flag, scripts crash on first prepared-statement reuse.
4. **No `hash()` for deterministic IDs**: Python's `hash()` is salted per-process. Use `hashlib.sha1` for advisory-lock-id derivation or anything needing cross-run determinism.
5. **Schema filters in validation queries**: production tables live in schema `mediator`, NOT `public`. `information_schema.columns WHERE table_schema = 'mediator'`.

## Stop conditions

- Any test that was passing on `main` starts failing — STOP, report.
- Any work item's executor decides the design is wrong and tries to redesign — STOP, report.
- Schema migration would touch existing rows destructively — STOP, report.
- Any artifact INSERT path can't fit `artifact_topics` write in the same transaction — STOP, report.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: S2a — Stamp insert sites, [REDACTED], observability, eval baseline

## Overview

S1 landed the additive schema (topics, bots, dyads, bindings, channels, identities, `artifact_topics`, `topic_status`, `user_bot_state`, nullable scope columns on `messages`/`bot_turns`/`scheduled_jobs`/`feedback`/`bridge_candidates`/artifact tables) and skeleton helpers (`routing.py`, extended `TurnContext` + `BotSpec`). S2a is the write-path pass.

**No root-cause re-think — the remaining 6 open precision flags are executor-precision details, not architectural problems.** v3 → v4 deltas close those last gaps:

- **correctness-1 / issue_hints / FLAG-016 (discord bot user id at runtime)**: `_decode_discord_user_id` lives in `scripts/seed_channels.py:55` (script-local) and is **not importable from `app/`**. Resolution: **promote** the helper to `app/services/discord_id.py` (new tiny module exposing `discord_bot_user_id() -> str | None`) that (a) reads `os.environ.get("DISCORD_BOT_USER_ID")` and (b) falls back to decoding `os.environ.get("DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN")` with the same base64url logic. Update `scripts/seed_channels.py` to import from the new module — keeps a single source of truth and avoids the `Settings.discord_bot_user_id` AttributeError. `app/config.py` does NOT need a new field for S2a.
- **correctness-2 (scheduler claim query)**: confirmed location — `app/services/scheduled_jobs.py:104-132` (`ScheduledJobs._claim_due_jobs`). The RETURNING list at lines 124-125 must add `sj.bot_id, sj.topic_id`. Dispatch is `scheduled_jobs.py:134` (`_dispatch`). All handlers live in `scheduled_job_handlers.py` (`handle_*`); grep-confirmed they take `job: dict[str, Any]`, no signature changes.
- **scope (withheld_reviews row stamps)**: `withheld_reviews` table is **out of scope for INSERT-column stamping in S2a** (not in the brief's table list). However the row INSERTs in `app/services/withheld_reviews.py:record_withheld_outbound_review` should accept `bot_id`/`topic_id` kwargs that get stored if/when the columns exist later. For S2a, thread the values through the function signature (no DB column change) so S2b can land the migration + lint rule without a second refactor. Document in `s2a_preflight.md`.
- **all_locations-1 (StampingFakePool fragility)**: simplified — the fake pool does **substring matching** only, not structural SQL parsing. Logic: if `"INSERT INTO " + <table>` appears AND `"INSERT INTO artifact_topics"` appears in the same SQL string, record both rows in the side dicts. Exact CTE wording is irrelevant.
- **all_locations-2 (hooks.py characterization)**: confirmed — `app/services/hooks.py` is 40 lines total. Only relevant code: `paused_for_user(user_id)` at `:37` and the `check_oob` hook indirection (`:13-28`). The "when one is in flight" phrasing is replaced with a concrete rule: **`paused_for_user` and `_default_check_oob` log invocations carry `extra={"user_id": ..., "bot_id": ...}` when those values are available at the call site (always for `check_oob`; for `paused_for_user`, pass `bot_id` through as a new optional kwarg since this function is consulted by the per-bot pause read-path in Step 12 anyway).** No fuzzy guards.
- **callers (TurnContext.dyad_id field placement)**: `dyad_id: UUID | None = None` is **appended at the end of the existing optional-fields block** in `app/services/turn_context.py` (after `cross_topic_policy: str | None = None` at line 33, before the existing-fields preserved block beginning at line 34). All existing positional-argument call sites (`tests/test_send_outbound.py:537,626,677,732`, `tests/test_llm_phase.py:73`, `tests/test_turn_plan.py:42,60,73`, `tests/test_evals_capture.py`) continue to work because they stop at the original positional fields and the new field has a default.

**Settled / not relitigated (per gate):**
- Dual-key coalescer transition stays dual (locked decision #1).
- `messages.channel_id` stays observability-only (no column, no migration).
- `bot_spec_version` = `sha1(repr(BotSpec))[:12]` intentionally tracks every dataclass field.
- Lint is advisory in S2a.
- Per-(user, bot) pause WRITE-path is deferred.

Hard guardrails (unchanged): no edits to the 9 pre-existing dirty files except the targeted-INSERT carve-out for `write_tools.py`; no edits to `docs/multi-agent-architecture.md`; no NOT NULL; no coalescer/suppression interface redesigns; STOP if any artifact INSERT can't fit `artifact_topics` in the same statement.

S1 lessons honored: `--help` smoke on every new CLI; `hashlib.sha1` (never `hash()`); `statement_cache_size=0` on new asyncpg connections; schema `mediator` for `information_schema`; SQL fragments sanity-checked against actual column types.

## Phase 0: Pre-flight — Lock baseline before any stamping

### Step 1: S1 parity + identity seeding pre-flight (`migrations/validation/s2a_preflight.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Verify** `SELECT count(*) FROM mediator.artifact_topics` returns **235**. If not, STOP.
2. **Verify** `mediator.user_identities` (non-legacy) and `mediator.channels` are seeded:
   ```sql
   SELECT count(*) FROM mediator.user_identities WHERE transport <> 'legacy';
   SELECT count(*) FROM mediator.channels;
   ```
   Both > 0. If empty in dev/CI, run `python scripts/seed_channels.py` first.
3. **Grep sweep** every INSERT under `app/` against target tables (`messages, bot_turns, scheduled_jobs, feedback, bridge_candidates, memories, themes, observations, watch_items, distillations, out_of_bounds, artifact_topics`). Write to `migrations/validation/s2a_insert_sites.md`.
4. **Direct-outbound audit**: grep for `INSERT INTO messages` filtered to `direction='outbound'` outside `messaging.py`. Expected empty; note in preflight doc; any hit added to the Step 12 gate list.

### Step 2: Capture eval baseline against pinned S1 tip (`tests/fixtures/eval_baseline/`, `scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** `scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py` in the main tree first — argparse with `--help`. Calls `evals.runner.run_scenarios` with an output collector; writes one JSON per scenario to `tests/fixtures/eval_baseline/<slug>.json` (scenario name, assistant outputs, tool-call sequence with redacted inputs, assertion verdicts, injected `now_utc`). Any asyncpg connection passes `statement_cache_size=0`. Do not modify the runner contract.
2. **Create worktree** at S1 tip: `git worktree add /tmp/veas-s1-tip d4c2a7c`.
3. **Copy** the new script into the worktree (it doesn't exist at `d4c2a7c`): `cp scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py /tmp/veas-s1-tip/scripts/`.
4. **Run** from inside the worktree: `cd /tmp/veas-s1-tip && python scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py`. Copy output back into the main tree.
5. **README** `tests/fixtures/eval_baseline/README.md`: commit SHA `d4c2a7c`, command, `now_utc`, scenarios captured.
6. **Cleanup** `git worktree remove /tmp/veas-s1-tip`.
7. **Smoke** `python scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py --help`.

## Phase 1: Wire routing into inbound; surface scope on TurnContext

### Step 3: Promote `_decode_discord_user_id` to a shared module (`app/services/discord_id.py`, `scripts/seed_channels.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `app/services/discord_id.py`:
   ```python
   import base64, os, re

   _DISCORD_BOT_USER_ID_RE = re.compile(r"^\d+$")

   def _decode_discord_user_id(token: str | None) -> str | None:
       if not token:
           return None
       try:
           segment = token.split(".", 1)[0]
           padded = segment + "=" * (-len(segment) % 4)
           decoded = base64.urlsafe_b64decode(padded).decode()
           return decoded if _DISCORD_BOT_USER_ID_RE.match(decoded) else None
       except (ValueError, UnicodeDecodeError):
           return None

   def discord_bot_user_id() -> str | None:
       explicit = os.environ.get("DISCORD_BOT_USER_ID")
       if explicit and _DISCORD_BOT_USER_ID_RE.match(explicit):
           return explicit
       return _decode_discord_user_id(os.environ.get("DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN"))
   ```
2. **Update** `scripts/seed_channels.py:55-90` to `from app.services.discord_id import _decode_discord_user_id`. Single source of truth; the seeder behavior is unchanged.
3. **No changes** to `app/config.py` Settings (avoids adding a field that would ripple through Pydantic validators).

### Step 4: Async startup hook for the relationship topic id (`app/bots/registry.py`, `app/main.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `populate_topic_ids_from_db(pool)` to `app/bots/registry.py`. Stores the UUID on a **module-level slot ONLY** — no `MEDIATOR_BOT` mutation, no `BotSpec` field:
   ```python
   _RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID: UUID | None = None

   async def populate_topic_ids_from_db(pool):
       global _RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID
       row = await pool.fetchrow("SELECT id FROM mediator.topics WHERE slug = 'relationship'")
       _RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID = row["id"] if row else None

   def get_relationship_topic_id() -> UUID | None:
       return _RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID
   ```
2. **Wire** the call into `app/main.py` startup right after `populate_mediator_spec_from_db` (both inside FastAPI lifespan).
3. **Fallback**: log a warning if the row is missing; the slot stays `None` (column is nullable per migration `0023`). Tests inject a UUID via `tests/_scope_helpers.py`.

### Step 9: Artifact writes + `artifact_topics` in same statement (`app/services/tools/write_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Large

> **Edit-safety contract for `write_tools.py`** (dirty file, INSERT-only carve-out):
> - Every change uses the `Edit` tool with a small unique `old_string` matching only the affected SQL fragment.
> - **Do not** delete or re-flow surrounding code, comments, or whitespace.
> - **Each `write_tools.py` change lands in its own commit** so accidental reflow is caught at review time.
> - After every edit, run `git diff --stat app/services/tools/write_tools.py` and confirm only additive changes appear at the targeted line range.
> - For existing multi-arm CTEs (`supersede_memory`, `revise_distillation`), **ADD a new arm** — never rewrite.

1. **Simple artifact INSERTs** (memories `:699`, themes `:764`, watch_items `:800`, observations `:878`, distillations `:939`, out_of_bounds `:1109`) rewrite from:
   ```sql
   INSERT INTO memories (...) VALUES (...) RETURNING id
   ```
   to:
   ```sql
   WITH new_artifact AS (
       INSERT INTO memories (..., recorded_by_bot_id) VALUES (..., $K) RETURNING id
   ), topic_link AS (
       INSERT INTO artifact_topics (artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id, tagged_by_bot_id, status)
       SELECT 'memories', new_artifact.id, $M, $K, 'active' FROM new_artifact
   )
   SELECT id FROM new_artifact
   ```
   `recorded_by_bot_id = ctx.bot_id`, `topic_id = ctx.primary_topic_id`.
2. **`supersede_memory` (`:737-757`)** — existing multi-arm CTE. **ADD** a third arm `, topic_link AS (INSERT INTO artifact_topics ... SELECT 'memories', new.id, $M, $K, 'active' FROM new)`. Keep `WITH old AS (UPDATE memories ...)` verbatim.
3. **`revise_distillation` (`:1041-1092`)** — existing multi-arm CTE (`old`, `new`, `revised_old`). **ADD** a fourth arm `, topic_link AS (INSERT INTO artifact_topics ... SELECT 'distillations', new.id, $M, $K, 'active' FROM new)`. Keep `revised_old` and the trailing `JOIN`/`SELECT`.
4. **STOP** and report if any artifact INSERT can't fit `artifact_topics` in the same statement. `_schedule_context_job` follow-ups on `watch_items`/`out_of_bounds` are intentionally separate (artifact + topic link atomic; scheduled-job side-effect outside) — DO NOT trigger STOP.

### Step 16: Tests + fixtures (`tests/_scope_helpers.py`, new `tests/test_s2a_*.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Do NOT edit** dirty test files. Create `tests/_scope_helpers.py` exposing:
   - `make_mediator_ctx(bot_spec=None, **overrides)` building a `TurnContext` with mediator defaults including `dyad_id`.
   - `make_resolved_scope(**overrides)` mock `ResolvedScope`.
   - **`StampingFakePool(FakePool)`** subclass — **substring matching only**, not structural SQL parsing. Pseudocode:
     ```python
     class StampingFakePool(FakePool):
         def __init__(self):
             super().__init__()
             self._artifacts: dict[str, list[dict]] = {}
             self._artifact_topics: list[dict] = []

         async def fetchrow(self, sql, *args):
             # Substring detection — does NOT require exact CTE wording.
             for table in ("memories", "themes", "observations",
                           "watch_items", "distillations", "out_of_bounds"):
                 if f"INSERT INTO {table}" in sql:
                     row = {"id": uuid4(), "args": args}
                     self._artifacts.setdefault(table, []).append(row)
                     if "INSERT INTO artifact_topics" in sql:
                         self._artifact_topics.append({"artifact_table": table,
                                                       "artifact_id": row["id"]})
                     return row
             return await super().fetchrow(sql, *args)
     ```
2. **Add** new test files:
   - `tests/test_s2a_stamping.py` — verifies `messages`/`bot_turns`/`scheduled_jobs`/`feedback`/`bridge_candidates` stamps including `bot_spec_version`/`hot_context_builder_version`/`tool_schema_version` (sha1, deterministic across two process runs) and `bridge_candidates.dyad_id`.
   - `tests/test_s2a_artifact_topics_same_stmt.py` — every artifact write produces an `artifact_topics` row via `StampingFakePool`.
   - `tests/test_s2a_coalescer_dual_key.py` — `(user_id, bot_id)` and `(user_id, None)` keys flush correctly.
   - `tests/test_s2a_suppression_dual_key.py` — EXISTS query includes `(bot_id = $ OR bot_id IS NULL)`.
   - `tests/test_s2a_pause_read_path.py` — `user_bot_state.paused=TRUE` withholds at messaging, scheduler dispatch, and at least one re-emission site (`withheld_reviews` or `pacer`).
   - `tests/test_s2a_scheduler_dispatch_bot_id.py` — instrumented handler asserts `job["bot_id"]` populated; checks the claim-query RETURNING list contains `bot_id, topic_id`.
   - `tests/test_s2a_discord_id_helper.py` — `discord_bot_user_id()` returns env var when set, falls back to decoded token, returns None when both empty. Confirms `_decode_discord_user_id` parity with the S1 seeder.
   - `tests/test_s2a_lint_inserts.py` — invokes `scripts/lint_inserts.py` against fixture strings.

## Execution Order
1. **Phase 0** first — eval baseline in the `d4c2a7c` worktree before any code change.
2. **Phase 1** (Steps 3–6) — discord_id helper, startup hook, `TurnContext.dyad_id`, routing wired into inbound, scope on every `TurnContext` site, `bot_turns` stamped.
3. **Phase 2** (Steps 7–10) — outbound, scheduler (claim query + handlers + seeds), artifacts (one commit per CTE rewrite/extend), bridge/feedback/withheld_reviews kwargs.
4. **Phase 3** (Steps 11–13) — coalescer dual-key, suppression dual-key, pause read-path with broader survey + gateway audit + `hooks.paused_for_user(bot_id=...)`.
5. **Phase 4** (Steps 14–17) — observability fan-out, linter, tests, final run + branch hygiene.

## Validation Order
1. After Step 2: `tests/fixtures/eval_baseline/` populated; worktree removed; `python scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py --help` parses.
2. After Step 3: `pytest tests/test_s2a_discord_id_helper.py -q` (or focused new test); the seeder script's import still works (`python scripts/seed_channels.py --help` if argparse, otherwise import-only smoke).
3. After Step 6: `pytest tests/test_agentic.py tests/test_inbound_source.py tests/test_routing.py -q`.
4. After Step 9: `pytest tests/test_s2a_stamping.py tests/test_s2a_artifact_topics_same_stmt.py -q`.
5. After Step 10: bridge / feedback / withheld_reviews tests pass.
6. After Step 11: `pytest tests/test_debouncer.py tests/test_s2a_coalescer_dual_key.py -q`.
7. After Step 12: `pytest tests/test_agentic_lifecycle.py tests/test_s2a_suppression_dual_key.py -q`.
8. After Step 13: `pytest tests/test_send_outbound.py tests/test_pause_resume.py tests/test_s2a_pause_read_path.py -q`.
9. After Step 15: `python scripts/lint_inserts.py app/` — iterate until empty for new code.
10. After Step 16: full `pytest -q` green.
11. Final: `python -m evals run --prompt-version v1` diff vs baseline; Step 17 branch hygiene verified.

(6) TurnContext.dyad_id placement spelled out: appended at end of the existing optional-fields block at line 33 (after `cross_topic_policy`), before the existing-fields preserved block at line 34 \u2014 preserves every positional constructor in non-dirty test files.         Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-002",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan\u2019s `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, but the 0020 migration defines the table with a standard `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)` constraint on raw columns. postgresql `on conflict` target lists must reference the exact column names of a unique constraint or a matching unique index; expression constructs like `coalesce` in the target list do not match a raw-column unique constraint and will raise a syntax or 'no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification' error at runtime. the seed script will fail unless either (a) the table is changed to use an expression-based unique index with those exact coalesce expressions, or (b) the seed script is rewritten to query-first/insert-if-missing or uses the plain column names and accepts the null-handling semantics of the constraint.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-011",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase b cte pair (`ins` and `src`) requires identical where/order/limit clauses for cursor correctness. both are written aligned but nothing enforces the invariant during executor edits; drift would silently mis-advance cursor.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-001",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the scripts/seed_channels.py plan uses on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing, but the 0020 migration creates a plain unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id) constraint. postgresql requires the on conflict target to match a unique constraint or index exactly; an expression-based target without a matching expression index will raise a runtime error. additionally, the raw unique constraint treats nulls as distinct, so it cannot deduplicate whatsapp channels where guild_id and channel_id are null. the executor must either add a matching unique expression index in 0020, or rewrite the seed script to use a pre-insert select check instead of on conflict.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-004",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the 0020 migration plan for user_identities omits the verified_at timestamptz column present in architecture doc \u00a712.2 ddl, while claiming 'exactly per \u00a73/\u00a712 ddl'. since account-linking workflows are deferred beyond s1, the column is not functionally load-bearing yet, but the claim of exact ddl match is incorrect.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-009",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the resolve_binding sql in app/services/routing.py uses limit 1 without order by. if a user ever has both a solo binding and a dyad binding to the same bot, the returned row is non-deterministic. the schema has no unique constraint preventing multiple bindings per (bot_id, user_id). since s1 only seeds one dyad binding and the module is not wired into inbound yet, this is latent future risk rather than an immediate s1 blocker.",

Read this file first — it contains 5 checks, each with a question and guidance.
For each check, investigate the codebase, then add your findings to the `findings` array for that check.

Each finding needs:
- "detail": what you specifically checked and what you found (at least a full sentence)
- "flagged": true if this describes a difference, risk, or tension — even if you think it's justified. false only if purely informational with no possible downside.
- Every check must end with at least one finding. Never leave a `findings` array empty. If you found no issue, add one detailed `flagged: false` finding explaining what you checked and why it appears clear.

When in doubt, flag it — the gate can accept tradeoffs, but it can't act on findings it never sees.

Good: {"detail": "Checked callers of nthroot_mod in solveset.py line 1205 — passes prime moduli only, consistent with the fix.", "flagged": false}
Good: {"detail": "The fix handles empty tuples but not single-element tuples which need a trailing comma.", "flagged": true}
Bad: {"detail": "No issue found", "flagged": false}  ← too brief, will be rejected
Bad: {"detail": "The hints suggest approach X but the plan uses Y. However Y is consistent with X's intent.", "flagged": false}  ← a different approach than the hints IS a flag. You found a divergence — flag it. The gate decides if it's acceptable.

After filling in checks, add any additional concerns to the `flags` array (e.g., security, performance, dependencies).
Use the standard format (id, concern, category, severity_hint, evidence). This array can be empty.

Workflow: read the file → investigate → read file again → add finding → write file back. Repeat for each check.

This is critique iteration 4. The template file includes prior findings with their status. Verify addressed flags were actually fixed, re-flag if inadequate, and check for new issues introduced by the revision.

        Additional guidelines:
        - Robustness level: standard. Use balanced judgment. Flag significant risks, but do not spend flags on minor polish or executor-obvious boilerplate.
        - Over-engineering: prefer the simplest approach that fully solves the problem.
        - Reuse existing flag IDs when the same concern is still open.
        - `verified_flag_ids`: list flag IDs from prior iterations that the revised plan actually resolves (e.g., if the plan was revised to fix FLAG-001, and you confirm the fix is correct, include "FLAG-001"). Only include flags you've verified — don't guess.
        - Verify that the plan follows the expected structure when validator warnings or the outline suggest drift.
        - Additional flags may use these categories: correctness, security, completeness, performance, maintainability, other.
        - Focus on concrete issues, not structural formatting.

cancelled=cancelled,

    async def _claim_due_jobs(self, *, now: datetime, heartbeat_only: bool) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
        rows = await self.pool.fetch(
            """
            WITH due AS (
                SELECT id
                FROM scheduled_jobs
                WHERE status = 'pending'
                  AND scheduled_for <= $1
                  AND ($3::boolean = false OR job_type = 'heartbeat')
                  AND (claimed_at IS NULL OR claimed_at < $1::timestamptz - interval '10 minutes')
                ORDER BY scheduled_for ASC, created_at ASC
                LIMIT $2
                FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED
            )
            UPDATE scheduled_jobs sj
            SET claimed_at = $1,
                claimed_by = $4,
                updated_at = $1
            FROM due
            WHERE sj.id = due.id
            RETURNING sj.id, sj.user_id, sj.job_type, sj.scheduled_for, sj.context,
                      sj.status, sj.attempt_count, sj.max_attempts, sj.delayed
            """,
            now,
            self.settings.scheduler_batch_size,
            heartbeat_only,
            self.worker_id,
        )
        return [dict(row) for row in rows]

    async def _dispatch(self, job: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
        handler = self.handlers.get(job["job_type"])

      40 app/services/hooks.py
---


def set_pool(pool: Any | None) -> None:
    global _pool
    _pool = pool


async def paused_for_user(user_id: UUID) -> bool:
    if _pool is None:
        return False
    return await system_state.is_paused(_pool)
---


@dataclass
class TurnContext:
    turn_id: UUID
    pool: Any
    user: User
    partner: User | None  # type-only change from User; NO default, position 4 preserved
    triggering_message_ids: list[UUID]
    # Sprint 1 new optional fields (all default to None, no call-site changes required)
    bot_id: str | None = None
    bot_spec: Any | None = None
    binding_id: UUID | None = None
    participants_shape: str | None = None
    primary_topic_id: UUID | None = None
    primary_topic_slug: str | None = None
    channel_id: str | None = None
    read_scopes: Any | None = None
    write_scopes: Any | None = None
    cross_topic_policy: str | None = None
    # Existing fields preserved in original order
    current_step: TurnStep = "respond"
    turn_plan: TurnPlan = field(default_factory=lambda: make_turn_plan("quick_reply"))
    tool_call_log: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    trigger_charge: str | None = None
    explicit_partner_alert_requested: bool = False
    turn_started_at: datetime | None = None

## S1 lessons the executor must honor

1. **Before declaring any new script/CLI complete**: actually invoke it with `--help` (or equivalent entry point) to confirm it parses and basic argparse setup works. Pytest passing is necessary but not sufficient — pytest may bypass `main()`.
2. **Any SQL fragment in a Python string**: dry-run via `EXPLAIN` against the live schema, or sanity-check the aggregate/operator types against actual column types (e.g. `max(uuid)` doesn't exist in Postgres).
3. **Any asyncpg connection** against the configured `DATABASE_URL`: pass `statement_cache_size=0` — Supabase transaction-mode pooler (port 6543) is what's configured. Without this flag, scripts crash on first prepared-statement reuse.
4. **No `hash()` for deterministic IDs**: Python's `hash()` is salted per-process. Use `hashlib.sha1` for advisory-lock-id derivation or anything needing cross-run determinism.
5. **Schema filters in validation queries**: production tables live in schema `mediator`, NOT `public`. `information_schema.columns WHERE table_schema = 'mediator'`.

## Phase 1: Wire routing into inbound; surface scope on TurnContext

### Step 3: Promote `_decode_discord_user_id` to a shared module (`app/services/discord_id.py`, `scripts/seed_channels.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `app/services/discord_id.py`:
   ```python
   import base64, os, re

   _DISCORD_BOT_USER_ID_RE = re.compile(r"^\d+$")

   def _decode_discord_user_id(token: str | None) -> str | None:
       if not token:
           return None
       try:
           segment = token.split(".", 1)[0]
           padded = segment + "=" * (-len(segment) % 4)
           decoded = base64.urlsafe_b64decode(padded).decode()
           return decoded if _DISCORD_BOT_USER_ID_RE.match(decoded) else None
       except (ValueError, UnicodeDecodeError):
           return None

   def discord_bot_user_id() -> str | None:
       explicit = os.environ.get("DISCORD_BOT_USER_ID")
       if explicit and _DISCORD_BOT_USER_ID_RE.match(explicit):
           return explicit
       return _decode_discord_user_id(os.environ.get("DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN"))
   ```
2. **Update** `scripts/seed_channels.py:55-90` to `from app.services.discord_id import _decode_discord_user_id`. Single source of truth; the seeder behavior is unchanged.
3. **No changes** to `app/config.py` Settings (avoids adding a field that would ripple through Pydantic validators).

### Step 4: Async startup hook for the relationship topic id (`app/bots/registry.py`, `app/main.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `populate_topic_ids_from_db(pool)` to `app/bots/registry.py`. Stores the UUID on a **module-level slot ONLY** — no `MEDIATOR_BOT` mutation, no `BotSpec` field:
   ```python
   _RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID: UUID | None = None

   async def populate_topic_ids_from_db(pool):
       global _RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID
       row = await pool.fetchrow("SELECT id FROM mediator.topics WHERE slug = 'relationship'")
       _RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID = row["id"] if row else None

   def get_relationship_topic_id() -> UUID | None:
       return _RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID
   ```
2. **Wire** the call into `app/main.py` startup right after `populate_mediator_spec_from_db` (both inside FastAPI lifespan).
3. **Fallback**: log a warning if the row is missing; the slot stays `None` (column is nullable per migration `0023`). Tests inject a UUID via `tests/_scope_helpers.py`.

This is append-only relative to the original positional fields — every existing test call site (e.g. `tests/test_send_outbound.py:537,626,677,732`, `tests/test_llm_phase.py:73`, `tests/test_turn_plan.py:42,60,73`) continues to work because they stop at the pre-S1 positional fields and the new field has a default.

Calls `resolve_bot` → `resolve_sender` → `resolve_binding`. On miss falls back to mediator defaults (`bot_id="mediator"`, `topic_id=get_relationship_topic_id()`, others `None`).

   Both populate `bot_id`, `bot_spec`, `binding_id`, `dyad_id`, `participants_shape`, `primary_topic_id`, `primary_topic_slug`, `channel_id`. Mediator-only defaults: `get_bot_spec("mediator")` + `get_relationship_topic_id()`. The consult sub-turn copies scope verbatim from its parent `ctx`.
2. **Stamp** `bot_turns` INSERT at `agentic.py:483` with:
   - `bot_id`, `topic_id` from `ctx`.
   - `bot_spec_version = hashlib.sha1(repr(ctx.bot_spec).encode()).hexdigest()[:12]` — intentionally tracks every dataclass field.
   - `tool_schema_version`: precompute at import as `hashlib.sha1(open(tool_schemas.__file__, 'rb').read()).hexdigest()[:12]` (reads `tool_schemas.py` bytes only; no edits to the dirty file).
   - `hot_context_builder_version`: `ctx.bot_spec.hot_context_builder_version`.
3. **Stamp** the deferred-turn `scheduled_jobs` INSERT at `agentic.py:577` with `bot_id`, `topic_id`.

## Phase 2: Stamp the remaining insert sites

### Step 7: Outbound message inserts (`app/services/messaging.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Thread** `bot_id`/`topic_id` from `TurnContext` into `_insert_outbound`; both INSERTs at `messaging.py:55` and `:84` add the columns.
2. **Thread** through `send_outbound` / `send_outbound_part` from call sites (agentic, scheduler, control paths). Where no `ctx` exists (`inbound._send_pause_confirmation`), pass `bot_id="mediator"` and `topic_id=get_relationship_topic_id()`.

### Step 8: Scheduler — claim query, dispatcher, seeds, reschedules (`app/services/scheduled_jobs.py`, `app/services/scheduled_job_handlers.py`, `app/services/checkins.py`, `app/services/tools/write_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Claim query** at `scheduled_jobs.py:104-132` (`ScheduledJobs._claim_due_jobs`): add `sj.bot_id, sj.topic_id` to the RETURNING list at lines 124-125. Verified location.
2. **Dispatch** at `scheduled_jobs.py:134` (`_dispatch`): no signature change needed — handlers in `scheduled_job_handlers.py:78,98,112,134,145,149,160` already take `job: dict[str, Any]` (grep-confirmed). `bot_id`/`topic_id` arrive as `job["bot_id"]`/`job["topic_id"]`.
3. **Heartbeat seed** `scheduled_jobs.py:216`: `bot_id='mediator'`, `topic_id=get_relationship_topic_id()`.
4. **Recurring scheduled_task reschedule** `scheduled_job_handlers.py:198-215` and weekly summary `:316-334`: read `job["bot_id"]`/`job["topic_id"]` from the originating row and re-stamp; mediator default if absent.
5. **Checkin seed** `checkins.py:37`: `bot_id`/`topic_id`.
6. **`_schedule_context_job` helper** `write_tools.py:160-191`: add `bot_id`/`topic_id` kwargs. Call sites (`add_watch_item`, `update_watch_item`, `add_oob`, `update_oob`) pass `ctx.bot_id`/`ctx.primary_topic_id`.
7. **`schedule_task`** `write_tools.py:1367`: `ctx.bot_id`/`ctx.primary_topic_id`.
8. **Supersede-and-insert helper** `write_tools.py:181-191`: accept `bot_id`/`topic_id` kwargs.
9. **Unit test** the dispatcher: instrumented handler asserts `job["bot_id"]` and `job["topic_id"]` are populated (`tests/test_s2a_scheduler_dispatch_bot_id.py`).

### Step 9: Artifact writes + `artifact_topics` in same statement (`app/services/tools/write_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Large

> **Edit-safety contract for `write_tools.py`** (dirty file, INSERT-only carve-out):
> - Every change uses the `Edit` tool with a small unique `old_string` matching only the affected SQL fragment.
> - **Do not** delete or re-flow surrounding code, comments, or whitespace.
> - **Each `write_tools.py` change lands in its own commit** so accidental reflow is caught at review time.
> - After every edit, run `git diff --stat app/services/tools/write_tools.py` and confirm only additive changes appear at the targeted line range.
> - For existing multi-arm CTEs (`supersede_memory`, `revise_distillation`), **ADD a new arm** — never rewrite.

1. **Simple artifact INSERTs** (memories `:699`, themes `:764`, watch_items `:800`, observations `:878`, distillations `:939`, out_of_bounds `:1109`) rewrite from:
   ```sql
   INSERT INTO memories (...) VALUES (...) RETURNING id
   ```
   to:
   ```sql
   WITH new_artifact AS (
       INSERT INTO memories (..., recorded_by_bot_id) VALUES (..., $K) RETURNING id
   ), topic_link AS (
       INSERT INTO artifact_topics (artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id, tagged_by_bot_id, status)
       SELECT 'memories', new_artifact.id, $M, $K, 'active' FROM new_artifact
   )
   SELECT id FROM new_artifact
   ```
   `recorded_by_bot_id = ctx.bot_id`, `topic_id = ctx.primary_topic_id`.
2. **`supersede_memory` (`:737-757`)** — existing multi-arm CTE. **ADD** a third arm `, topic_link AS (INSERT INTO artifact_topics ... SELECT 'memories', new.id, $M, $K, 'active' FROM new)`. Keep `WITH old AS (UPDATE memories ...)` verbatim.
3. **`revise_distillation` (`:1041-1092`)** — existing multi-arm CTE (`old`, `new`, `revised_old`). **ADD** a fourth arm `, topic_link AS (INSERT INTO artifact_topics ... SELECT 'distillations', new.id, $M, $K, 'active' FROM new)`. Keep `revised_old` and the trailing `JOIN`/`SELECT`.
4. **STOP** and report if any artifact INSERT can't fit `artifact_topics` in the same statement. `_schedule_context_job` follow-ups on `watch_items`/`out_of_bounds` are intentionally separate (artifact + topic link atomic; scheduled-job side-effect outside) — DO NOT trigger STOP.

### Step 10: Bridge candidate + feedback inserts; withheld_reviews kwarg pass-through (`app/services/tools/write_tools.py`, `app/services/discord.py`, `app/services/withheld_reviews.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **bridge_candidates** INSERT at `write_tools.py:305-329`:
   - `bot_id = ctx.bot_id`
   - `topic_id = ctx.primary_topic_id`
   - `dyad_id = ctx.dyad_id` (NOT `ctx.binding_id` — `bot_bindings.id` vs `bot_bindings.dyad_id` are distinct).
   Use the Step 9 edit-safety contract.
2. **Feedback INSERTs:**
   - `write_tools.py:1763` (`log_feedback`): `bot_id=ctx.bot_id`, `topic_id=ctx.primary_topic_id`.
   - `discord.py:468-478` reaction handler: resolve scope via the **bot's** address (matches `channels.address` per `scripts/seed_channels.py:95-101`):
     ```python
     from app.services.discord_id import discord_bot_user_id
     bot_user_id = discord_bot_user_id()
     scope = await _resolve_scope(self.pool, 'discord', bot_user_id) if bot_user_id else None
     bot_id = scope.bot_id if scope else 'mediator'
     topic_id = scope.topic_id if scope else get_relationship_topic_id()
     ```
     Use the resolved values in the feedback INSERT. Never the reacting user's id.
3. **withheld_reviews row INSERTs** (`record_withheld_outbound_review` in `app/services/withheld_reviews.py`): **thread `bot_id`/`topic_id` through the function signature as new optional kwargs**, but do NOT change the SQL column list in S2a — the table has no scope columns yet. Callers (`messaging.py:207-216, 342-365`) pass them. This unblocks S2b's migration + lint-rule extension. Document in `s2a_preflight.md`.

## Phase 3: Dual-key rekey + per-(user,bot) pause read paths

### Step 11: Dual-key BurstCoalescer (`app/services/debouncer.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Extend** `_Burst` with optional `bot_id: str | None = None`.
2. **Rekey** internal dicts to composite tuple keys `(user_id, bot_id_or_None)`. Lookups try the composite first, fall back to `(user_id, None)` (locked decision #1). Internal `AsyncBurstCoalescer` (third-party) remains keyed by `user_id`; bot-aware dispatch happens at `_fire_batch`.
3. **Add** `bot_id` kwarg to `BurstCoalescer.add(...)` (default `None`), propagate to `_Burst`.
4. **Mark** each fallback site with `# TODO(S2b): drop legacy (user_id, None) fallback`.

**Scope:** Small
1. **Modify** `_newer_inbound_exists` at `agentic.py:592-625`: add `AND (bot_id = $4 OR bot_id IS NULL)` to the EXISTS query, bind `ctx.bot_id` as a new parameter.
2. **Same** at `read_tools.py:152-179`.

### Step 13: Per-(user, bot) pause read path — broader survey + gateway audit (`app/services/system_state.py`, `messaging.py`, `scheduled_job_handlers.py`, `oob_check.py`, `withheld_reviews.py`, `recovery.py`, `pacer.py`, `discord.py`, `whatsapp.py`, `hooks.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** `user_bot_paused(pool, user_id, bot_id) -> bool` in `system_state.py` (reads `user_bot_state.paused`).
2. **Add** an optional `bot_id` kwarg to `hooks.paused_for_user` (`app/services/hooks.py:37`). Callers in `messaging.py:170,335` pass `ctx.bot_id`. Internal implementation may treat `None` as global pause only — but having the parameter is the readiness step for S2b.
3. **Gate sites:**
   - `messaging.py:170` (`send_outbound_part`), `:335` (`send_outbound`): withhold if `is_paused OR user_bot_paused(pool, user.id, bot_id)`.
   - `scheduled_job_handlers.py` dispatch (`scheduled_jobs.py:134`-side or in each handler's entry): gate before fire.
   - `oob_check.py`: user-visible release.
   - `withheld_reviews.py`: released-review emissions.
   - `recovery.py:86,114`: re-injects via `coalescer.add` / `add_burst`; downstream turn goes through `send_outbound` → gated centrally. Annotate with `# pause-check via send_outbound`.
   - `pacer.py`: paced answer/reaction emissions.
   - `discord.py`/`whatsapp.py`: grep-confirmed all outbound routes through `messaging.send_outbound`/`send_outbound_part`. Annotate with `# pause-check N/A: routes through send_outbound`.
4. **Audit method**: grep each file for `send_outbound`, `send_outbound_part`, or direct outbound INSERTs. Each site either passes a gate OR carries a `# pause-check N/A: <reason>` comment. STOP if structurally hard.
5. **Write path stays deferred**: no code writes to `user_bot_state.paused` in S2a. Document at the top of `system_state.py`.

## Phase 4: Observability, lint, tests, validation

**Scope:** Medium

Where no `ctx` exists, build scope from local `bot_id`/`channel_id` or call `_resolve_scope` once at function top.

### Step 15: Advisory linter for INSERTs (`scripts/lint_inserts.py`, CI workflow)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** `scripts/lint_inserts.py` — argparse `--help`. Walks `app/` files; scans SQL string literals matching `INSERT INTO (messages|bot_turns|scheduled_jobs|feedback|bridge_candidates)`; flags missing `bot_id`/`topic_id` (and `dyad_id` for `bridge_candidates`).
2. **Same** scanner flags `INSERT INTO (memories|themes|observations|watch_items|distillations|out_of_bounds)` whose enclosing SQL string lacks an `INSERT INTO artifact_topics` clause (substring check, not structural).
3. **CI step**: `lint-inserts` runs `python scripts/lint_inserts.py || true` — exit always 0 in S2a. `# TODO(S2b): make blocking` comment in the script.
4. **Smoke** `python scripts/lint_inserts.py --help`. Output: `path:line: kind: missing column 'bot_id'`.

### Step 16: Tests + fixtures (`tests/_scope_helpers.py`, new `tests/test_s2a_*.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Do NOT edit** dirty test files. Create `tests/_scope_helpers.py` exposing:
   - `make_mediator_ctx(bot_spec=None, **overrides)` building a `TurnContext` with mediator defaults including `dyad_id`.
   - `make_resolved_scope(**overrides)` mock `ResolvedScope`.
   - **`StampingFakePool(FakePool)`** subclass — **substring matching only**, not structural SQL parsing. Pseudocode:
     ```python
     class StampingFakePool(FakePool):
         def __init__(self):
             super().__init__()
             self._artifacts: dict[str, list[dict]] = {}
             self._artifact_topics: list[dict] = []

         async def fetchrow(self, sql, *args):
             # Substring detection — does NOT require exact CTE wording.
             for table in ("memories", "themes", "observations",
                           "watch_items", "distillations", "out_of_bounds"):
                 if f"INSERT INTO {table}" in sql:
                     row = {"id": uuid4(), "args": args}
                     self._artifacts.setdefault(table, []).append(row)
                     if "INSERT INTO artifact_topics" in sql:
                         self._artifact_topics.append({"artifact_table": table,
                                                       "artifact_id": row["id"]})
                     return row
             return await super().fetchrow(sql, *args)
     ```
2. **Add** new test files:
   - `tests/test_s2a_stamping.py` — verifies `messages`/`bot_turns`/`scheduled_jobs`/`feedback`/`bridge_candidates` stamps including `bot_spec_version`/`hot_context_builder_version`/`tool_schema_version` (sha1, deterministic across two process runs) and `bridge_candidates.dyad_id`.
   - `tests/test_s2a_artifact_topics_same_stmt.py` — every artifact write produces an `artifact_topics` row via `StampingFakePool`.
   - `tests/test_s2a_coalescer_dual_key.py` — `(user_id, bot_id)` and `(user_id, None)` keys flush correctly.
   - `tests/test_s2a_suppression_dual_key.py` — EXISTS query includes `(bot_id = $ OR bot_id IS NULL)`.
   - `tests/test_s2a_pause_read_path.py` — `user_bot_state.paused=TRUE` withholds at messaging, scheduler dispatch, and at least one re-emission site (`withheld_reviews` or `pacer`).
   - `tests/test_s2a_scheduler_dispatch_bot_id.py` — instrumented handler asserts `job["bot_id"]` populated; checks the claim-query RETURNING list contains `bot_id, topic_id`.
   - `tests/test_s2a_discord_id_helper.py` — `discord_bot_user_id()` returns env var when set, falls back to decoded token, returns None when both empty. Confirms `_decode_discord_user_id` parity with the S1 seeder.
   - `tests/test_s2a_lint_inserts.py` — invokes `scripts/lint_inserts.py` against fixture strings.

### Step 17: Validate + branch hygiene
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** `pytest -q` — green. If any S1-passing test fails, STOP.
2. **Re-run** `python -m evals run --prompt-version v1`; diff against `tests/fixtures/eval_baseline/`. Note drift in the PR description.
3. **Branch hygiene**: `git diff --stat <s1-tip>..HEAD -- <each dirty file>`. The 8 non-`write_tools.py` dirty files show zero changes; `write_tools.py` shows only additive INSERT-localized changes. `docs/multi-agent-architecture.md` not committed.
4. **Confirm** each `write_tools.py` commit is independently atomic.

## Execution Order
1. **Phase 0** first — eval baseline in the `d4c2a7c` worktree before any code change.
2. **Phase 1** (Steps 3–6) — discord_id helper, startup hook, `TurnContext.dyad_id`, routing wired into inbound, scope on every `TurnContext` site, `bot_turns` stamped.
3. **Phase 2** (Steps 7–10) — outbound, scheduler (claim query + handlers + seeds), artifacts (one commit per CTE rewrite/extend), bridge/feedback/withheld_reviews kwargs.
4. **Phase 3** (Steps 11–13) — coalescer dual-key, suppression dual-key, pause read-path with broader survey + gateway audit + `hooks.paused_for_user(bot_id=...)`.
5. **Phase 4** (Steps 14–17) — observability fan-out, linter, tests, final run + branch hygiene.

## Validation Order
1. After Step 2: `tests/fixtures/eval_baseline/` populated; worktree removed; `python scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py --help` parses.
2. After Step 3: `pytest tests/test_s2a_discord_id_helper.py -q` (or focused new test); the seeder script's import still works (`python scripts/seed_channels.py --help` if argparse, otherwise import-only smoke).
3. After Step 6: `pytest tests/test_agentic.py tests/test_inbound_source.py tests/test_routing.py -q`.
4. After Step 9: `pytest tests/test_s2a_stamping.py tests/test_s2a_artifact_topics_same_stmt.py -q`.
5. After Step 10: bridge / feedback / withheld_reviews tests pass.
6. After Step 11: `pytest tests/test_debouncer.py tests/test_s2a_coalescer_dual_key.py -q`.
7. After Step 12: `pytest tests/test_agentic_lifecycle.py tests/test_s2a_suppression_dual_key.py -q`.
8. After Step 13: `pytest tests/test_send_outbound.py tests/test_pause_resume.py tests/test_s2a_pause_read_path.py -q`.
9. After Step 15: `python scripts/lint_inserts.py app/` — iterate until empty for new code.
10. After Step 16: full `pytest -q` green.
11. Final: `python -m evals run --prompt-version v1` diff vs baseline; Step 17 branch hygiene verified.

        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-002",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan\u2019s `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, but the 0020 migration defines the table with a standard `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)` constraint on raw columns. postgresql `on conflict` target lists must reference the exact column names of a unique constraint or a matching unique index; expression constructs like `coalesce` in the target list do not match a raw-column unique constraint and will raise a syntax or 'no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification' error at runtime. the seed script will fail unless either (a) the table is changed to use an expression-based unique index with those exact coalesce expressions, or (b) the seed script is rewritten to query-first/insert-if-missing or uses the plain column names and accepts the null-handling semantics of the constraint.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-011",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase b cte pair (`ins` and `src`) requires identical where/order/limit clauses for cursor correctness. both are written aligned but nothing enforces the invariant during executor edits; drift would silently mis-advance cursor.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
